# Reef Rush handoff

## User intent

The user asked for one original, public, desktop-first browser game inspired by the quality of the Astra game projects. The selected concept is **Reef Rush**, a solo third-person underwater time-trial game with three courses. The repository must remain public under `ridermw/reef-rush` and eventually deploy through GitHub Pages at the `/reef-rush/` base path.

On September 4, 2026, the user asked to stop implementation, push all work, and preserve enough context to resume on another device.

Shoals is registered in the explicit dynamic import map; other course IDs

- Matching schema, loader, current, gate, and lifecycle tests.

Course runtimes own only their resources, not the caller's world. Cleanup is
idempotent and retryable. **Task 8 callers must retain a thrown
`ConstructionCleanupError` until `retryCleanup()` or `dispose()` succeeds**;
the error owns resources whose rollback failed, including partial gate bodies.
Do not replace it with a message string and lose that owner.

The first independent Rubber Duck review identified lost ownership after failed
construction cleanup and fractional current boundary exclusions. Both were
reproduced with failing assertions and fixed. The followup review cleared spec
compliance and quality with no Critical or Important findings. This used one
generic reviewer context per review round, the default model, and high effort
requested; there is no model diversity or consensus claim.

### Task 7: race rules and versioned progression

Commit `d8ee4f0` adds immutable race state and events, ordered oriented checkpoint
crossings, swept pearls, active timing, inclusive medals, independently retained
best records, chained course unlocks, and protected version 1 browser saves.
Only Sunlit is available; eligibility does not imply that later content exists.

Important files:

- `src\game\race\RaceSession.ts`, `medals.ts`, and `raceTypes.ts`
- `src\game\race\raceGeometry.ts`, `raceIntersection.ts`,
  `exactArithmetic.ts`, and `exactSphereEntry.ts`
- `src\game\progression\progress.ts`
- `src\game\save\progressStorage.ts`
- Matching race, geometry, medal, progression, storage, and course schema tests.

Repeated independent review exposed premature floating point rounding in
geometry, timing, and uncertainty bookkeeping. The final design retains exact
intersection data for contact eligibility, ordering, and duration scaling.
Full active time and input uncertainty accumulate in fixed dyadic millisecond
units. Only the terminal finish uses a rational fraction, so denominators do not
grow with every frame.

Coordinates are exact binary64 inputs. Each supplied duration has an allowance
of half its input ULP. Medals compare the exact lower interval endpoint against
course thresholds; public elapsed time rounds separately. Standalone
`awardMedal` remains strict. Four former nominal boundary fixtures actually
exceeded their thresholds even after that allowance. They remain rejection
regressions; inclusive partition fixtures use exact integer coordinates.

Storage uses `reef-rush.progress`, validates before writing, preserves invalid
or unsupported existing saves, and reports acquisition and write failures.
It is not called automatically by the race. Recovery UI remains Task 13.

The final independent Rubber Duck review cleared spec and quality with zero
Important or Critical findings. Each review round used one independent generic
reviewer context with the default model and high effort requested; there is no
model diversity or consensus claim.

Parent `npm run validate` passed 22 Vitest files with 669 tests, 3 bootstrap
tests, formatting, linting, types, asset validation, and build. Hosted run
`33932822991` passed on `d8ee4f0`. An implementation microbenchmark measured a median
13.02 microseconds per generated Sunlit race step versus 10.51 before the exact
clock change. That is a headless Node probe, not browser performance or human
gameplay evidence. Task 8 supplies the production shell connection.

## Decisions and rulings

- TypeScript is pinned to 6.0.3 because `typescript-eslint` 8.68.0 does not support TypeScript 7.
- The committed npm lockfile contains public registry URLs and SHA-512 integrity values. Direct access to `registry.npmjs.org` was blocked on the original device, so local package refreshes used the available proxy without committing proxy URLs.
- `W/S` control throttle; arrow up/down control pitch. This avoids coupling forward speed to pitch.
- The React shell must stay independent from the frame-by-frame game runtime.
- Both Sunlit Shoals glTF outputs now exist and are validated before accepting
  the original scene. Later courses must preserve this visual/collision gate.
- Task 13 owns invalid-save recovery UI; Task 10 owns general shell polish.
- A document-wide CSS reset remains a deferred minor review note.

## Remaining plan

1. Task 14: finish normal production browser coverage, shared artifacts and pinned CI using the reviewed plan and time allocations below.
2. Task 15: finish public documentation, provenance, GitHub Pages workflow, deployment and canonical live verification.

The original detailed plan and design were stored in the first device's Copilot session folder, not the repository. This handoff preserves their load-bearing decisions and task sequence in a receiver-visible file.

### Slice 13A: graphics lifecycle and explicit retry

**Goal:** interrupted graphics must not silently consume race time. Restore the
same retained run in a paused state, with an explicit fresh course retry when
needed. Unsupported graphics must show actionable guidance and clean ownership.

**Choice:** use the existing renderer creation/failure boundary rather than
allocate a separate graphics probe. Retaining the paused scene on context loss
preserves the attempt; always discarding it would avoid restoration rather than
implement it. A separate renderer/framework or automatic resume is out of scope.

**Files:** `src\app\screens.ts`, `src\app\appStore.ts`,
`src\app\App.tsx`, `src\app\components\ErrorScreen.tsx`,
`src\app\components\PauseScreen.tsx`, `src\app\components\GameHud.tsx`,
and `src\game\core\GameHost.ts`.
Coverage belongs in the existing store, shell, and host Vitest files and
`tests\browser\graphics-recovery.spec.ts`.

- [x] Add and observe failing store/host/shell regressions. Introduce a
      `graphicsLost` state flag and guarded `GRAPHICS_LOST`, `GRAPHICS_RESTORED`,
      and `RETRY_COURSE` actions. Loss pauses a playing race, preserves paused and
      finished races, and never clears earned progress or a finished result.
      Restoration clears the flag without resuming. Loading/replay/title reset
      the flag; resume is forbidden while graphics remain lost.
- [x] Bind native canvas loss/restoration listeners to renderer ownership,
      not temporary surface attachment. Prevent default loss handling, stop the
      frame chain, clear timing/input, pause audio and the active race, and retain
      scene resources. Old or released renderer events must not change a new run.
- [x] On actual restoration, resize and redraw the retained scene, reset the
      timing baseline, and remain paused until an explicit resume. No catchup
      steps, fresh scene, duplicate listeners, or extra frame chain may appear.
      Cover repeated events, detachment, title/disposal, and queued saves.
- [x] Add an explicit `GameHost.retryCourse()` entry point for an error with a
      selected course or a paused run with lost graphics. Retry pending cleanup
      only because the user requested it, then dispatch a fresh load. A cleanup
      failure must remain visible and retain its owner; do not construct another
      scene until cleanup succeeds. Preserve invalid call guards.
- [x] Wire native retry buttons and clear graphics guidance into the existing
      shell. Disable resume while lost, including Escape; keep settings/title
      available. Explain that retry restarts the current attempt, not saved
      progress. Initial WebGL failure must explain the WebGL 2/current desktop
      browser/hardware acceleration requirement without claiming every load error
      has that cause.
- [x] Add headless browser coverage for unavailable WebGL, real
      `WEBGL_lose_context` loss/restoration, frozen timing during loss, explicit
      resume, fresh retry, and teardown. Use actual graphics events, not synthetic
      DOM events or mutable game hooks. Preserve settings/progress and exact scene
      ownership. Existing loading cancellation, hidden tab pause, and error
      recovery cases must continue to pass.

Run the affected existing Vitest files in one invocation, then types and lint.
Run `npm run test:browser -- "graphics-recovery|lifecycle|native-focus"
--project=acceptance` headlessly, without racing a build. Restore the normal
production output afterward. Review spec and quality before publication.
Full validation and the complete browser suite remain the Task 13 milestone
gate after all four slices; earlier expansion is warranted by a concrete failure.

| Field      | Initial graphics experiment                                                                                                                                        |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Baseline   | Source inspection finds no host owned WebGL lifecycle listeners or direct error retry. The completed baseline passes 1,516 unit/integration cases and 28 browsers. |
| Hypothesis | Explicit graphics lifecycle events can stop time and preserve the retained scene, while guarded retry reuses the existing cleanup and load queue.                  |
| Metric     | Zero steps during loss, same race/scene on restoration, no automatic resume, one frame chain after explicit resume, clean retry, and zero owners after teardown.   |
| Result     | Parent 149 focused tests, types, lint, eight native browser cases, and the independent review pass. The nested diagnostic finding is repaired.                     |
| Decision   | Keep the reviewed graphics behavior; no performance score or GPU reclamation claim.                                                                                |

### Slice 13B: explicit protected save recovery

Source inspection confirms that `progressStorage.ts` exposes guarded reads
and ordinary writes, not a reset API. Do not weaken `saveProgress` or add an
automatic fallback. Recovery needs a separate, explicit path.

**Design:** expose a native saved progress dialog from stationary shell screens,
not an interactive overlay during play/loading. Reuse the existing modal input,
focus, and Escape behavior. A small shared modal helper is preferable to copying
the Settings dialog's lifecycle/keyboard implementation.

- The dialog reads a fresh storage snapshot and clearly distinguishes valid,
  empty, malformed, unsupported, and unavailable storage. Never derive these
  states by parsing the human notice string.
- Offer a local backup download for invalid raw data. Use a JSON envelope
  containing the exact raw JavaScript string so even unmatched surrogate code
  units survive a round trip. Render preview text safely and bound its length;
  no raw data goes to logs, diagnostics, or a network service. Revoke owned
  object URLs when the dialog replaces its snapshot or closes.
- Unsupported versions remain protected: backup and guidance only, with no
  replacement action in this older game version.
- For malformed JSON or invalid version 1 schema, require a separate explicit
  acknowledgment before enabling replacement. Explain that the original save
  is replaced with progress actually earned in the current session, not silently
  reset on load. Keep a backup available before confirmation.
- Add a separate `replaceInvalidProgress` storage operation. Validate its
  replacement progress, obtain storage once, and require that the current read
  is still a replaceable invalid save with the exact raw value the player
  inspected. Do not clear the key before writing. Any valid, empty, changed,
  unsupported, unavailable, or failed write outcome must be explicit.
- GameHost queues the operation with its existing save work and acquires the
  same `reef-rush.progress` Web Lock. Reread under the lock, use the latest
  session progress there, and do not await between comparison and write.
  Concurrent cooperative writers and late earned records must be preserved.
  A failed recovery must not poison later queued saves or look successful.
- A changed save cancels replacement and requires a fresh inspection and
  confirmation. Valid/unavailable states can explicitly retry ordinary saving
  through the existing merge and invalid save safeguards. No import/editor,
  valid progress reset, extra backup storage key, or automatic retry loop.
- Preserve pending saves across navigation/disposal. Closing the dialog
  discards its authorization and restores focus; it must never resume a race.
  A replacement still waiting for the queue or lock must recheck cancellation
  before writing. Ordinary race saves remain durable; an already committed
  replacement must never be reported as rolled back merely because the dialog
  closed afterward.
  Keep the saved progress control stable on stationary screens so successful
  recovery does not remove the focus return target.

Implement this after 13A's publication gate. Storage, host queue, and native
dialog regressions must fail before their changes. Cover stale raw data, a
new valid save while waiting for the lock, unsupported versions, denied storage,
write failures, exact backup round trips, cancellation, and current session
record preservation. Browser evidence must include a real finish against an
invalid save followed by explicit recovery, and a real competing tab/Web Lock.
No actual user save outside the isolated test browser contexts is modified.

**Implementation and parent evidence:** the storage helper
validates the replacement and exact inspected raw value, protects every other
read outcome, and performs one guarded write. Host recovery shares the ordinary
save queue and native Web Lock, uses current session progress inside the lock,
and distinguishes cancellation before commit from a committed replacement.
Explicit ordinary retry returns its actual outcome without weakening invalid
save protection. `NativeDialog.tsx` owns the common Settings/recovery lifecycle;
`SavedProgressDialog.tsx` adds backup, bounded preview, acknowledgment, fresh
inspection, and cancellation. App navigation clears obsolete recovery dialogs.

The implementation observed eighteen new storage failures with forty four
existing cases passing, fifteen host failures with eighty nine existing cases
passing, and eighteen shell failures before their corresponding fixes. Native
coverage first failed on the missing entry control. A later failing navigation
regression exposed a recovery dialog reopening after returning to title; the
screen scoped modal state repairs that lifetime defect.

Initial parent validation passed 218 tests across `progressRecovery`, `progressStorage`,
`GameHost`, `SavedProgressDialog`, and `App`, plus types, scoped lint, formatting,
and all eleven selected recovery, race storage, and shell feedback browser cases.
The normal production output was restored and excludes the acceptance global.

| --------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Actual finish and replacement     | 23,103.12315668551 ms, bronze, all four checkpoints and pearls. The unchanged Sunlit driver used recovery waypoint five. The original invalid raw value survived until acknowledged replacement, and reload retained the actual earned result.                     |
| Downloaded backup                 | The malformed save envelope was 97 bytes and retained all twenty two raw code units, including two unmatched surrogate units. The future version envelope was 102 bytes and retained all thirty six raw code units.                                                |
| Competing writer                  | An actual finish at 24,287.920151349015 ms earned bronze with all four checkpoints and pearls and no recovery waypoint. A newer valid save won the native competing lock; stale recovery left it unchanged, and explicit ordinary retry merged the session record. |
| Cancellation and unsupported data | Closing removed the native pending lock request. Releasing and reacquiring that lock did not replace the original. Unsupported data exposed backup and guidance only.                                                                                              |
| Modal ownership                   | Paused native controls, Tab/Escape, focus return, race retention, Settings persistence, audio ownership, replay, and course navigation pass.                                                                                                                       |

| Field      | Protected save experiment                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline   | Ordinary saves preserve invalid raw storage but there is no player recovery control. Parent baseline source is `0dab1fe`.                                                                                 |
| Hypothesis | Explicit exact snapshot consent and the existing save lock can recover actual session progress without overwriting changed, valid, or future data.                                                        |
| Metric     | Exact backup round trip, one authorized write at most, zero writes after precommit cancellation or stale inspection, preserved concurrent records, truthful feedback, and retained modal input ownership. |
| Result     | Parent evidence passes 226 focused tests, eleven initial native cases and all five recovery cases after review repairs. Both scoped reviewers cleared their findings.                                     |
| Decision   | Keep and publish the reviewed recovery behavior. No human accessibility or browser compatibility beyond the exercised Chromium cases is claimed.                                                          |

Two fresh generic Rubber Duck contexts reviewed separate persistence and
consent/modal scopes, using high effort requested and no model override:
`rubber-duck-save-persistence` and `rubber-duck-save-consent`. The review path
used two independent contexts, not three; model diversity was unconfirmed.
The scopes were complementary, so no consensus ranking or combined vote is
claimed. Each found one medium issue, which the parent reproduced before fixing:

- Ordinary retry returned private writer error causes containing raw saves.
  Six failing cases covered malformed, invalid schema, and future data at both
  the initial host read and the defensive writer reread. Public retry failures
  now retain a leaf error without raw cause graphs; the initial error retains
  the invalid reason. Storage guards and explicit failure outcomes stay intact.
- Download backup remained active while replacement waited for the lock. A
  failing held operation case reproduced a late backup failure alongside an
  outstanding authorization. Backup is now disabled while busy. A separate
  already passing fault case locks in backup failure blocking confirmation
  until an explicit successful backup retry.

All 226 focused tests, types, scoped lint, and formatting pass after these
repairs. All five native recovery cases pass again, including the new disabled
backup assertion while a real native lock is pending. The final actual recovery
finish was 27,329.39393847305 ms/bronze; the competing writer finish was
23,153.466811798677 ms/bronze. Both retained all four checkpoints and pearls
and used the unchanged Sunlit driver's recovery waypoint five.
Each original reviewer cleared its repair in a bounded followup, not a new
independent vote. Normal production output was restored without acceptance
hooks. Source `8992752` is published on the feature branch.

### Slice 13C: persisted render quality

**Goal:** offer predictable graphics cost choices without changing simulation,
course visibility rules, accessible shell resolution, or the current default.
The existing autonomous authorization applies. Implementation starts only after
13B publication and this plan's independent readiness gate.

**Choice:** use explicit render resolution presets, not automatic adaptation or
asset/effect replacement. Automatic adaptation introduces timing dependent
behavior and unstable measurements. Changing decoration budgets expands scene
ownership and presentation contracts without being necessary for a useful
resolution control. All original assets and reduced motion rules stay unchanged.

**Architecture:** keep the existing Settings store and native modal. Add
`renderQuality: 'low' | 'medium' | 'high'` to strict settings version two.
High is the default and preserves `Math.min(2, devicePixelRatio)`. Medium and
low multiply that ratio by `0.75` and `0.5`, respectively, on every display.
Only the canvas drawing buffer changes; CSS size, camera aspect, input, race,
and physics remain unchanged. This bounds pixel count to approximately 56.25
percent and 25 percent of high, subject to integer canvas dimensions.

**Storage choice:** use an explicit version two schema rather than silently
changing strict version one's shape. Accept only the exact existing version one
schema as legacy input, normalize it in memory with `renderQuality: 'high'`,
and preserve every preference. Reading must not write or rewrite raw storage.
An explicit settings update saves version two through the existing guarded
whole configuration writer. Invalid legacy, malformed, and future versions
remain preserved; there is no settings recovery or automatic migration write.
Version two still requires every field, rejects unknown keys and invalid enum
values, and cannot be selected through a settings patch.

**Files:** `src\settings\settings.ts`, `src\settings\settingsStorage.ts`,
`src\game\rendering\renderQuality.ts`, `src\game\core\GameHost.ts`, and
`src\app\components\SettingsDialog.tsx`. Use existing settings/store/host/App
tests, add `tests\unit\renderQuality.test.ts`, and add
`tests\browser\render-quality.spec.ts`. Update typed fixtures and existing
literal settings assertions only where the new explicit schema requires it;
retain legacy fixtures that establish compatibility.

- [x] Add failing schema/storage/store regressions for all three choices,
      unchanged high defaults, exact version one normalization, no write on
      read, explicit version two persistence, invalid patch/enum rejection,
      protected future version three, and session only settings on write failure.
      Run the three existing settings unit files and observe those failures
      before implementing strict normalization and guarded persistence.
- [x] Add a pure renderer ratio mapping with tests that fail before its code.
      Its contract is `renderPixelRatio(dpr: number, quality: RenderQuality)`:
      validate a finite positive DPR, return `Math.min(2, dpr)` times the
      selected scale, and reject invalid inputs rather than produce NaN.
      Cover DPR one, two, above the cap, fractional values, and invalid values.
- [x] Add failing host regressions for initialization, actual quality changes,
      resize, retained pause, pending graphics loss/restoration, explicit retry,
      and teardown. Apply the latest quality at renderer sizing and update the
      retained surface when quality changes. Avoid a new renderer, scene, input
      binding, physics world, frame chain, or React frame subscription. Changes
      to volume or unrelated preferences must not reallocate the drawing buffer.
      Preserve the existing visible resize error handling and zero size guard.
      Fault inject measurement, `setPixelRatio`, and `setSize` failures through
      a quality update, not just ResizeObserver. Route that path through the
      existing guarded resize callback and assert visible error/frame shutdown,
      continued notification of other Settings subscribers, and truthful saved
      versus session only status. Do not broaden the Settings store catch.
- [x] Add a failing native Settings control regression, then a labeled select
      with High, Medium, and Low options. Explain that the control changes game
      resolution, not shell text or simulation. Show the existing storage notice
      when the selected preference can apply only in this session. Preserve
      modal keyboard ownership, focus return, and effective reduced motion.
- [x] Add headless browser cases before completing the wiring. At DPR one and
      two, measure real CSS and drawing buffer dimensions for each choice,
      retain the same paused canvas/pose/race/resources, and prove reload
      persistence. Use a real graphics loss/restoration cycle to prove a choice
      made during loss applies on restoration without resuming. Preserve the
      progress raw value, native input ownership, and reduced motion behavior.
- [x] Run the affected unit files together, types, scoped lint/format, and
      `npm run test:browser -- "render-quality|graphics-recovery|shell-feedback"
--project=acceptance`. Do not race shared builds. Restore normal output,
      obtain one independent implementation gate, record measured results here,
      and publish the reviewed slice before diagnostics work.

| Field      | Initial quality experiment                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline   | Every scene uses `Math.min(2, dpr)` and settings version one has no render quality preference.                                                                                                             |
| Hypothesis | Explicit resolution scales can lower drawing buffer pixel count while retaining the exact run and original scene ownership.                                                                                |
| Metric     | High matches baseline; medium and low use the specified dimensions at DPR one and two; legacy preferences survive; paused and lost runs do not advance; persistence and reduced motion remain independent. |
| Result     | Parent evidence passes 305 focused plus 98 existing consumer tests, types, scoped lint/format and all ten selected native cases. Independent implementation review cleared the slice without findings.     |
| Decision   | Keep and publish the measured rendering behavior. Do not infer frame rate or human visual quality from the pixel count formula.                                                                            |

One fresh generic Rubber Duck plan reviewer, with high effort requested,
accepted the storage and quality contract but identified a medium test matrix
gap: a settings triggered resize could bypass the existing guarded observer
entry point and interrupt subscriber notification. The plan now explicitly
requires fault injection through that path and reuse of the guarded callback.
This is a readiness clarification, not an observed production defect. The
review used one independent context; model diversity and consensus are not
claimed. The 13B prerequisite is complete; candidate evidence follows below.

**Implementation evidence:** strict version two settings
and the separate legacy read schema preserve exact version one preferences
without writing on read. The pure ratio helper validates enum/DPR values and
rejects a scaled zero from numeric underflow. GameHost caches the selected
quality and invokes its existing guarded resize callback only when it changes.
Native Settings now exposes the labeled select. No scene, physics, asset,
input, save, or frame chain ownership was added.

The implementation observed twelve settings regressions, fifteen executable
ratio failures after the missing module boundary, eleven host failures, three
UI failures, and three native missing control failures before the respective
changes. The host regressions include all six resize failure/persistence status
combinations required by the plan review.

Parent validation passes 305 cases in six files, types, scoped lint, formatting,
and ten native graphics, quality, and shell feedback cases. The persisted native
report has ten expected results, zero unexpected/flaky/skipped results, and no
report errors. All three quality evidence attachments are present.
An additional 98 existing audio engine and protected recovery dialog cases
also pass against version two settings; neither consumer required changes.

### Slice 13D: bounded diagnostics and measured lifecycle

**Goal:** expose useful local troubleshooting evidence without a live React
frame subscription, unbounded histories, save disclosure, or telemetry. Measure
the exercised lifecycle and performance rather than assign a graphics score.
Source `ca74a3c` is reviewed, published and integrated on main. The complete
Task 13 local, native and hosted gates are recorded below.

**Choice:** add a fixed capacity frame sampler and a manually refreshed native
diagnostics dialog. Reuse existing scene/audio ownership counts and
`NativeDialog`. Add an explicit safe production projection rather than copying
the full acceptance snapshot. A live overlay would couple shell rendering to measurement;
remote telemetry, heap profiling services, GPU timers, benchmark packages, asset
changes, and automatic quality adaptation are outside this slice.

**Measurement contract:**

- `src\game\core\frameMetrics.ts` owns three preallocated numeric windows of
  120 samples: runner input interval milliseconds for sampled playing frames, synchronous host work
  milliseconds, and simulation milliseconds discarded by `FixedStepRunner`.
  `createFrameMetrics()` exposes `record`, `reset`, and `getSnapshot`.
  Recording is constant space/time, without sorting or shifting. Reject invalid
  numeric samples before mutation. Do not retain frame snapshots, errors,
  closures, DOM nodes, raw saves, or a growing event list.
- Summaries are immutable and computed only on explicit reads: capacity,
  sample count, number of samples with dropped time, and mean/P95/maximum for
  each window. P95 uses nearest rank. Empty summaries use null, not misleading
  zero performance or NaN. Include extreme finite inputs in finite-summary
  regressions and use numerically safe aggregation. Never expose mutable backing arrays.
- Integrate through `GameHost.onFrame` and the existing monotonic clock. Use
  the actual interval already passed to the runner, before its cap, and its
  returned `droppedSeconds`. Time synchronous simulation/presentation/render
  submission work, not asynchronous GPU completion or total browser work.
  Record only successful rendered frames that remain in the playing state.
  Paused, hidden, lost, zero size, failed, stale, and disposed callbacks must not
  add samples or change the simulation/scheduling contract.
- Reset the window on a fresh course load and an actual render quality change,
  so statistics never mix presets or attempts. Retain the most recent window
  through pause, context loss, title, and failure for inspection. Resuming uses
  the existing timing reset, never the paused wall interval. Disposal stops all
  collection. Lifetime rendered/step counters remain unchanged and distinct.
  Add a lifetime `frame.profiled` counter to the existing immutable acceptance
  snapshot; increment only after a qualifying sample. This cheap scalar permits
  native observation without computing statistics on every acceptance read.
  The full summaries belong to the separate explicit diagnostic read.

**Safe read boundary:** `GameHost.getDiagnostics(): HostDiagnostics` constructs
an allowlisted primitive/numeric projection before copying or freezing. It
must not call the existing full `getSnapshot`, read/expire run feedback,
traverse raw error/cause graphs, or enumerate arbitrary audio metadata.
The audio snapshot contains opaque causes and errors, so select only its
trusted status/phase/context flags and ownership counts. Report cleanup status
and pending counts without walking new error graphs; existing error presentation
remains unchanged. Add cyclic metadata and throwing getter regressions, plus
repeated reads proving no feedback/gameplay mutation or store notifications.

**UI contract:** add `DiagnosticsDialog.tsx` with one initial safe diagnostic read and an
explicit Refresh button. The Diagnostics entry shares the stationary shell
utility row with Saved progress and is absent during playing/loading. Extend
the existing single modal state and screen scoped invalidation; opening,
closing, Tab, Escape, navigation, and focus return retain the same ownership.
Use labeled text/definition lists in the current palette, not a live chart.
Show screen/lifecycle, graphics status, current quality and drawing buffer,
recent running samples, active scene counts, canvas/frame-chain/pending owners,
and bounded cleanup/audio status. Label active resources separately from
pending owners. Explain that measurements are local and manually refreshed,
CPU work is not GPU time, and these counts do not prove driver reclamation.
No storage/error objects, raw text, account/browser fingerprints, export service,
clipboard permission, automatic logging, network requests, or new window hook.

```text
successful playing render -> record interval/work/discarded time -> 120 slots
fresh load / changed preset -------------------------------------> reset
pause / loss / title / failure ----------------------------------> retain
native Diagnostics / Refresh -> safe projection + summaries -> static UI
close / navigation -> common modal cleanup; no frame subscription
```

**Files:** new `src\game\core\frameMetrics.ts` and
`src\app\components\DiagnosticsDialog.tsx`; integrate in
`src\game\core\GameHost.ts`, `src\app\App.tsx`, and `src\styles\app.css`.
Add `tests\unit\frameMetrics.test.ts`, `tests\unit\DiagnosticsDialog.test.tsx`,
and `tests\browser\diagnostics.spec.ts`; extend existing host/App tests and
`tests\browser\production.spec.ts`. Update typed snapshot fixtures as required,
without adding mutable acceptance controls or changing course drivers.

- [x] Observe failing sampler regressions for empty/one/exact quantiles, invalid
      inputs without partial mutation, overwrite after many multiples of 120,
      reset, extreme finite summaries, and immutable independent reads; then implement.
- [x] Observe failing host regressions with controlled clocks and real runner
      behavior. Cover dropped time, pause/hidden/loss, stale callbacks, zero
      dimensions, render failure, resume, new load, changed/unchanged quality,
      title, and disposal. Assert existing step/frame chains and shell
      notifications remain unchanged; collection must not publish React state.
      Prove paused renders cannot increment `frame.profiled`. Cover the safe
      projection against opaque cyclic causes and throwing metadata getters,
      without changing the existing full snapshot contract beyond the scalar.
- [x] Observe failing UI cases for stationary gating, empty samples, truthful
      units/labels, explicit refresh only, bounded safe projection, shared modal
      lifecycle, focus, navigation, and no automatic resume; then wire the
      production dialog and utility row.
- [x] Exercise native inspection and actual graphics loss/restoration. After
      running frames, pause and open Diagnostics; compare displayed values with
      expected unit contracts and the immutable snapshot's ownership/counters,
      prove no live refresh, and retain the race on
      Escape. Test empty/reset samples after a quality change and fresh load.
- [x] Run a fixed native matrix at a recorded viewport and DPR: all three
      original courses at each of the three presets, one attempt per cell,
      on the same host/page so retained owners can accumulate if cleanup fails.
      Use a 960 by 540 CSS viewport and DPR one, a bounded test local time
      budget, and the same neutral native input sequence for every cell.
      Seed valid qualification only as an isolated lifecycle fixture, not as
      evidence of earned progression. Wait for 180 rendered playing frames so
      the 120 sample window excludes the first sixty warmup frames. Record
      actual mean/P95/maximum intervals and CPU work, dropped samples, and
      drawing buffer dimensions. Never rerun a cell to improve its number.
      Do not use the existing RAF-polled `frames()` helper unchanged. Observe
      `frame.profiled` at a disclosed 200 ms cadence, immediately reject an
      interrupted/non-playing attempt, and record an actual qualifying delta
      of at least 180. Paused/results renders cannot meet this condition.
      Pause before opening the native Diagnostics UI for the final summary,
      so sorting and rendering the report do not contaminate the measured
      window. Keep summaries out of the ordinary acceptance snapshot.
      These are observations near the beginning of each course, not a full
      traversal benchmark or a worst case performance guarantee.
      Return to title between cells; verify the same per-course active owner
      counts and zero active scene/canvas/frame-chain/pending owners afterward.
      This exercises three complete ownership cycles for each course.
- [x] Extend the normal production browser case to open/refresh/close
      Diagnostics through native controls while `__REEF_RUSH_TEST__` remains
      absent. Rename its old "no diagnostics" wording to "no acceptance global";
      do not weaken lazy loading, real gameplay, framebuffer, or cleanup checks.
- [x] Run affected Vitest files together, types, scoped lint/format, then
      `npm run test:browser -- "diagnostics|lifecycle" --project=acceptance`
      and the affected production case. Restore normal output. Obtain one
      independent implementation gate and publish measured evidence.
- [x] Finish Task 13 with `npm run validate`, the complete browser suite,
      normal output restoration, independent milestone gate as warranted, and
      the hosted source baseline. Integrate into main only after those outcomes
      are recorded; then proceed to Task 14's production browser CI.

| Field      | Initial diagnostics experiment                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| ---------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline   | Immutable snapshots expose ownership and lifetime frame counts, but no bounded performance window or production inspection dialog. Existing native lifecycle coverage repeats Sunlit three times.                                                                                                          |
| Hypothesis | Constant capacity local sampling and manual inspection can expose useful performance and ownership evidence without adding frame subscriptions or retaining run resources.                                                                                                                                 |
| Metric     | Exactly bounded 120 sample retention; correct synthetic statistics and dropped time; no samples outside successful playing frames; stable active per-course owners and zero active scene/canvas/frame-chain/pending owners after every matrix cell; no storage disclosure or production acceptance global. |
| Result     | Repaired source passes 204 parent focused cases, full 1,714 case validation, all 41 native browser cases and hosted source baseline 33975373933. Every native matrix cell met the bounded sampling and ownership contracts; normal emitted JavaScript has no acceptance global.                            |
| Decision   | Keep the corrected bounded observation without implicit suspension. One independent reviewer cleared the repair and quality stage. Timings are local observations, not a portable frame rate threshold or human graphics score; instrumentation overhead is not independently isolated.                    |

The first independent plan pass identified two medium gaps. The existing full
snapshot traverses opaque audio metadata and expires feedback, so it is not
the production inspection boundary. The plan now requires an allowlisted
`getDiagnostics` projection before copying, with no feedback read or raw error
graph. The native matrix also now uses a cheap qualifying frame counter and
200 ms observation rather than repeated full statistic reads on every RAF.
One separate generic Rubber Duck reviewer cleared those two plan repairs in an
informed followup. Both contexts used high effort requested with no model
override; model diversity was unconfirmed. The second pass was a repair check,
not another independent finding or consensus vote. Implementation was reviewed
separately below.

### Completed diagnostics implementation review repair

One independent generic Rubber Duck reviewer found that an initial early return
for hidden or zero sized surfaces reset and bypassed the runner. Before this
slice, a playing frame still consumed simulation/input and published its normal
presentation state, with size gating only presentation/rendering. The candidate
also had later hidden returns that could stop the frame chain.
Its initial zero-step unit expectation enshrined this unintended behavior.

The parent confirmed the source difference. Eight new compatibility regressions
failed before the repair. Eligibility now gates sampling only; excluded frames
preserve baseline runner, input/pause, HUD updates, fractional time, rendering
and scheduling behavior without adding samples. Actual visibility events retain
automatic pause and explicit resume. The parent independently passed all 204
host, fixed step, input and sampler cases after the repair.

The complete browser run before repair passed all 41 cases in 1,142.19 seconds
of outer wall time, including startup/builds and shutdown. All 24 attachments
are preserved, including six originally backed by temporary files, and all nine
matrix cells met their qualification/ownership contracts. This remains baseline
evidence, not the final repaired source gate. The repaired source separately
passed full validation and all 41 browser cases in 1,126.56 seconds.

The reviewer used one fresh context with high effort requested and no model
override or confirmed provider diversity. The same reviewer cleared the repair
and completed the quality stage without further findings. This was an informed
followup, not another independent review or consensus.

### Final diagnostics and milestone evidence

The final matrix used one page/host, one attempt per cell, a 960 by 540 CSS
viewport and DPR one. Qualification was an isolated fixture, not earned-chain
evidence. Actual qualifying deltas were 180 to 186; the retained 120 samples
excluded 72 to 80 observed warmup frames. Requested polling cadence was 200 ms,
with actual gaps of 202.9 to 337.9 ms. Summaries were read from the paused native
dialog and rounded to two decimals.

| Course / preset      | Qualifying delta | Interval mean / P95 / max (ms) | CPU work mean / P95 / max (ms) |
| -------------------- | ---------------- | ------------------------------ | ------------------------------ |
| Sunlit / Low         | 186              | 30.28 / 33.50 / 50.00          | 2.61 / 4.00 / 13.80            |
| Sunlit / Medium      | 184              | 34.16 / 50.00 / 50.10          | 2.06 / 3.00 / 3.70             |
| Sunlit / High        | 185              | 38.47 / 50.10 / 66.70          | 1.58 / 2.30 / 3.30             |
| Kelpworks / Low      | 181              | 36.53 / 50.00 / 50.10          | 2.71 / 3.50 / 7.70             |
| Kelpworks / Medium   | 180              | 51.53 / 66.70 / 66.80          | 2.54 / 3.60 / 6.80             |
| Kelpworks / High     | 183              | 67.36 / 83.40 / 83.40          | 2.13 / 2.90 / 3.50             |

This final window had zero discarded time and zero dropped samples in every
cell. Earlier observations did include dropped samples. Differences are not
causal proof of a speed improvement and were not used to select a passing
score. Backing dimensions were 480 by 270, 720 by 405, and 960 by 540.

Bodies/colliders/geometries/materials remained 1/7/29/27 for Sunlit,
2/10/31/32 for Kelpworks and 3/13/35/38 for Blacksmoker across all three cycles.
Each title return had zero active scene, canvas, frame chain and pending release
owners. The shared audio context remained owned by design. These early course
observations do not prove worst case performance, GPU completion, driver memory
reclamation, or human graphics/gameplay quality.

The complete final browser report has 41 expected results, no unexpected,
flaky or skipped outcomes, no report errors and 24 preserved attachments.
Actual malformed/future backup files were 97/102 bytes with 22/36 raw UTF-16
code units. Normal production output was restored. Hosted run `33975373933`
on `ca74a3c` passed all 1,714 tests across 51 files, bootstrap, types, lint,
formatting, original asset validation and build. It took 123 seconds and did
not run browsers. Main contains that exact source, not an untested merge tree.

## Task 14: production browser CI

**Goal:** make the actual local acceptance gates reproducible on hosted
Windows, retain useful failure evidence, and cover all original courses in
the normal production build. The reviewed implementation is on the feature
branch; passing hosted acceptance and main integration remain required.

**Choice:** extend `.github\workflows\windows-baseline.yml` rather than create
a duplicate validation workflow or a new orchestration layer. Preserve its
manual invocation and public dependency recovery role. Add pull requests and
pushes to main, one Windows 2022 job with a forty five minute outer budget,
and the complete existing browser suite. No deployment permissions or Pages
publication belong to this task.

### Runtime and workflow contract

The following release commits and their `action.yml` declarations were checked
through GitHub's API. All three declare the Node 24 action runtime; the project
runtime must still come from `.nvmrc`, not that bundled action runtime.

| Action                    | Release  | Verified immutable commit                  |
| ------------------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `actions/checkout`        | `v7.0.1` | `3d3c42e5aac5ba805825da76410c181273ba90b1` |

### Runner deadline and collection budget

The outer forty five minute job timeout is not the browser suite deadline.
Before implementing the workflow, record the completed Task 13 full browser
wall time, including its builds, and choose explicit numeric phase allocations
from that observation and the hosted baseline. Run `33962260364` took 126
seconds overall: checkout six, runtime selection seven, dependency restore
seventeen, manual cache export five, and validation eighty seven seconds.
These are observations, not a guarantee about the new browser phase or a cold
Chromium installation.

Bound preceding workflow steps, including manual cache export. Give Playwright
its own CI only `globalTimeout`, preserving the unrestricted local default and
all individual case budgets. Reserve at least five minutes after that runner
deadline for reporter completion, normal rebuild when appropriate,
artifact upload, and post steps. The browser step's hard timeout must be later
than the internal deadline, while the sum of phase bounds and collection room
must remain inside the outer budget. Record the resulting numeric allocation
table before implementation; confirm cold installation and all phase timings
in the first hosted run. If measured scope cannot fit with useful headroom,
revise the outer budget explicitly rather than omit cases or weaken assertions.

Exercise a controlled browser failure or runner suite timeout and confirm
that nonpassing outcomes and available JSON/HTML/trace evidence remain
discoverable. An `always()` upload alone is not proof of orderly shutdown.
The runner reuses its deadline for teardown, so a suite timeout can leave
unfinished case statuses and incomplete ZIP archives even when JSON/HTML
reporting completes. Retain available raw partial traces/resources under hidden
result directories. Hard cancellation, runner loss, or a hung reporter can
still prevent collection; do not promise complete teardown or trace finalization.

The original allocation used Task 13 measurements: 1,126.56 seconds for the
repaired suite and 1,142.19 seconds for the earlier complete candidate.
Both include builds, startup and shutdown.
Its hosted baseline `33975373933` took 123 seconds: checkout six, runtime
selection four, dependency restore fourteen, manual cache export five and
validation ninety seconds. Task 14 has since observed Chromium installation
in fifteen to sixteen seconds. Its latest complete 43 case run took 1,357.66
seconds and fits the unchanged suite bound, but three medal assertions still
fail. These timing observations do not establish a passing acceptance gate.

| Phase                                   | CI bound                              |
| --------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------- |
| Checkout                                | 1 minute                              |
| Runtime selection                       | 1 minute                              |
| Bootstrap and locked restore            | 2 minutes                             |
| Manual cache export, when applicable    | 1 minute                              |
| Full validation                         | 3 minutes                             |
| Chromium installation                   | 2 minutes                             |
| Browser suite runner deadline           | 28 minutes, `globalTimeout: 1680000`  |
| Browser step hard deadline              | 29 minutes, including the suite above |
| Normal rebuild and emitted output check | 1 minute                              |
| Browser evidence upload                 | 2 minutes                             |
| Job setup allowance                     | 1 minute                              |
| Post steps and remaining slack          | 2 minutes                             |
| Whole job                               | 45 minutes                            |

Preceding step bounds sum to ten minutes, plus the job setup allowance.
The runner browser limit is about
47 percent above the slower observed complete local run, without reducing any
case allowance. Within these phase allocations, six minutes remain after the
runner deadline: one for shutdown/reporting, one for normal output, two for
upload and two for post steps/slack. The hard browser step remains
strictly later than the internal limit. This is an initial measured allocation,
not a guarantee of hosted speed; retain the controlled timeout exercise and
adjust explicitly from real evidence if necessary. Local `globalTimeout` stays
zero, and retries, workers, assertions and course thresholds stay unchanged.

### Stable artifact ownership

Current `playwright.config.ts` uses `process.pid` in its temporary root.
Launcher and worker configuration evaluation can therefore put downloads and
reports in different roots, as directly observed during Task 13.

Add an explicit `REEF_RUSH_BROWSER_ARTIFACTS` override. When present, reject
blank values and resolve it to one shared absolute root; append the existing
`results` and `production-dist` child directories. When absent, generate the
isolated local temporary/PID root once in the launcher, then propagate the
resolved value through that process's environment to its children. This retains
local invocation isolation without preserving the worker split. Do not modify
the user's persistent environment. The workflow supplies
`${{ runner.temp }}\reef-rush-browser-${{ github.run_id }}-${{ github.run_attempt }}`
in the browser and collection step environments. Keep both declarations
identical: `runner` is not available in job environment expressions, and
collection must resolve the path even if earlier steps failed. All browser
child processes inherit the browser step value. Do not add a worker PID below it.

On CI, configure list, JSON and HTML reporters, with `results.json` and a
`report` directory under that root and HTML `open: 'never'`. Local defaults
remain the list reporter. Upload only `results`, `results.json`, and `report`.
No tracked build output, broad directory deletion, new dependency, or new
testing framework is needed.

```

### Task 14 bounded control reproduction phase

The fresh diagnosis confirmed two distinct mechanisms rather than a scoring
defect. At the recorded step 785, the policy selects only W because it
minimizes final yaw/pitch error. Its own forecast passes the pearl plane at
z=64, reaches z=70.75, and stays about 1.506 units above the target. The actual
combined pickup radius is 0.7. The objective does not assess interception or
velocity. Separately, the recorded steps 832 through 869 match 29 neutral
physics steps followed by eight Down steps, not 37 steps of immediately
applied Down. Pointwise rollouts and canned observations exclude this delayed
feedback behavior.

The chosen prerequisite was reproduction, not a new winning controller. A direct
pointwise objective tune is smaller but would still omit observation age.
Bypassing normal input instrumentation could reduce latency but would alter
evidence and leave the interception defect. The chosen approach is a bounded,
deterministic replay through the existing real `SceneRuntime` and `RaceSession`,
with generated observations from the evolving simulation and explicit held
inputs during observation/release/press delays.

Existing fixture interfaces are suitable: `createAssetCache` with
`localAssetLoader` reads the committed GLBs; `SceneRuntime.start`, `step`,
`getSnapshot` and `dispose` preserve real currents, obstacles, collision and
race awards. Snapshots copy/freeze player state. Reuse the 1/60 step constant
and bounded traversal style, but do not substitute the existing analog
traversal controller for binary keyboard inputs or native earned proof.
The existing original asset/physics sentinel still passes at 1,317 steps,
21,940.483 ms, all four checkpoints/pearls and bronze. That is fixture
validation, not the delayed native replay result.

The completed timing analysis cannot identify a faithful phase resolved
schedule from the existing three traces. RPC completion is not input
application. Add the bounded observational instrumentation below before
finalizing the replay schema; do not invent application steps or independently
assign phase maxima that share one observation gap.

The replay must start from spawn and award every milestone through actual
movement. The policy sees only copied observations, not the simulator's
current hidden state. Track actual race time, pickup/crossing steps, swept
pearl clearance and recoveries. Use explicit finite iteration limits and
dispose every scene. First reproduce the unchanged controller's failure; if
that does not reproduce, repair the model before changing the controller.
Only a later reviewed phase may implement a stateful interception policy.
Acceptance remains every original milestone and a real finish at or below
30,000 ms, followed by the unchanged native/hosted gates. No runtime behavior, asset,
route, medal, retry, deadline or evidence reduction is authorized.

### Native input timing implementation plan

> Execute this bounded prerequisite inline with the executing plans workflow
> after its independent readiness gate. Do not implement a replacement
> controller or guessed replay schedule in this slice.

**Goal:** obtain actual browser observation and native key event stamps in
physics steps without changing gameplay or input delivery.

**Architecture:** the existing acceptance hook gains one cheap, immutable
counter/gating projection inside its existing build flag. A bounded passive browser
recorder stamps existing observations and movement key events. A test fixture
drains and attaches evidence once, preserving operation and cleanup errors.
Only the first Sunlit traversal in the three currently failing earned scenarios
enrolls the recorder.

**Stack:** existing TypeScript, Vitest, Playwright, GameHost and Node core only.
No package, workflow, timeout, retry, viewport or trace option changes.

The timing analysis found 304 cycles and 96 empty transitions across the three
latest first Sunlit traversals, with neutral prefixes of 172, 114 and 121
physics steps. It found 32 conditional pitch timing anchors, but not a faithful
phase resolved schedule. Server action end precedes after snapshot capture
and client response. Wall milliseconds cannot simply be divided by 16.667
to obtain physics steps. Unknown nonempty phase durations remain unknown,
not zero. The selected instrumentation supplies effective observation to key
event offsets; it does not claim to decompose client age and phase settlement
without additional clock brackets.

export type NativeTimingEvent =
  | (InputStamp & {
      kind: 'observation';
      sequence: number;
      time: number;
      anchor: NativeObservationAnchor;
    })
  | (InputStamp & {
      kind: 'key';
      sequence: number;
      time: number;
      type: 'keydown' | 'keyup';
      code: string;
      repeat: boolean;
      isTrusted: boolean;
      defaultPrevented: boolean;
      canvasTarget: boolean;
      altKey: boolean;
      ctrlKey: boolean;
      metaKey: boolean;
    });

export interface NativeTimingData {
  readonly version: 1;
  readonly events: readonly NativeTimingEvent[];
  readonly failure: string | null;
}

export interface NativeInputRecorder {
  observe(state: NativeObservedState): void;
  finish(): Promise<NativeTimingData>;
}

export type NativeObservedState = Pick<
  HostSnapshot,
  'screen' | 'frame' | 'player' | 'race' | 'collectedPearlIds' | 'preferences'
>;

export interface NativeObservationAnchor {
  readonly player: HostSnapshot['player'];
  readonly courseId: string | null;
  readonly elapsedMs: number | null;
  readonly checkpointIndex: number | null;
  readonly pearlCount: number | null;
  readonly status: string | null;
  readonly collectedPearlIds: readonly string[];
  readonly mouseSteering: boolean;
}
```

counter only data lacked replay anchors when a successful trace is discarded,
and trusted events alone did not establish input eligibility or reset history.

ambiguity: a blanket results exemption could hide earlier resets. The exact
single terminal increment rule and its two interruption regressions now close
that gap. No other blocking contradiction was reported. This was one reviewer
with informed followups, not new independent consensus.

### Baseline replay design boundary

The next deliverable is a falsifiable baseline reproduction, not a replacement
controller. Replaying recorded observations into the policy alone cannot
validate evolving physics. Replaying only `stepFishMotion` omits actual
collision, currents and race awards. Use the existing real `SceneRuntime`
and original committed GLBs, with two explicitly separated modes:

1. Motion calibration replays the measured effective input tape and compares
   intermediate states. A known synthetic binary schedule, labeled synthetic,
   independently establishes the completed step convention and comparison
   tolerances before the hosted data is used. The reference path lives in the
   integration test and drives a separate real scene with literal normalized
   input frames per step. It must not call the replay scheduler, corpus parser,
   edge adapter or its generated anchors.
2. Baseline reproduction additionally generates the existing waypoint and
   keyboard policy decisions from the evolving simulation. Each generated
   release/press set must match the measured delivery slots before those
   generated inputs are applied. Recorded fish state is an oracle for
   assertions only, never a state reset or policy observation. Matching must be
   bijective: each generated native code/type edge owns exactly one slot, with
   identical cardinality. Set equality alone must not hide duplicate edges.

The measured data identifies effective native events and returned observation
times in physics steps. It does not identify client acknowledgement phases.
Do not simulate those phases by pretending native event time is RPC completion.
Within a cycle, check that every recorded release precedes replacement presses;
preserve measured delivery order within a phase rather than assuming a batch
is atomic. An empty transition must match an unchanged generated held set.

This tape is specific to the unchanged baseline's command topology. A future
controller that requests different keys cannot be qualified by pretending it
would receive these exact slots or observation delays. Such a controller needs
a separate explicit model and native qualification after this prerequisite.

Planned file boundaries:

- `tests\fixtures\nativeTimingCorpus.ts`: strict corpus validation and loading.
- `tests\fixtures\native-timing\sunlit-33991547417.json`: generated, portable
  evidence containing the three actual first Sunlit tapes and provenance.
- `tests\fixtures\replaySunlitTiming.ts`: bounded physical replay, intermediate
  comparison and baseline decision matching.
- `tests\unit\nativeTimingCorpus.test.ts`: malformed and mismatched corpus
  regressions.
- `tests\integration\nativeSunlitReplay.test.ts`: independent binary
  calibration, measured baseline fidelity, failure reproduction and mutation
  negatives.
- This handoff: concrete implementation steps, observed results and next gate.

The corpus must retain the actual decoded timing values, source revision
`87ef5f47a73b642d52078ebf7dbd1baef7734aae`, run `33991547417`, artifact
`9977067136`, scenario identities and hashes of the original timing bodies.
Generate it from the preserved report, not copied console summaries. Exclude
machine paths, environment data, traces and storage. Keep all three actual
failures; do not select a more convenient subset.

Validate before creating a scene: bounded record count, finite counters,
clock and motion values, ordered sequences, eligible trusted native keys,
the exact clear history, correlated course/milestone/pearl identities, and
valid observation/key ordering. Missing, malformed or interrupted evidence
must fail explicitly. The known three cases require their real terminal
anchors and no remaining held keys.

Start each runtime at the original spawn through `runtime.start()`. Advance
the actual neutral prefixes of 148, 116 and 150 steps; never seed their
already earned checkpoints or pearls. Use the existing fixed 1/60 interval
and 7,200 step limit. For an event stamped `k`, complete step `k` under the
previous input before delivering the event. Multiple events at `k` execute
in recorded sequence with no fabricated physics step between them.
Preserve the total sequence across keys and observations sharing a step.
Blacksmoker record 201, step 1,868, is an existing key after an observation at
that same step and must not be regrouped ahead of it.

Reuse `advanceSunlitWaypoint`, `sunlitSteeringTarget`,
`courseKeyboardPolicy`, the original asset loader and existing input mapping.
The baseline policy sees only copied snapshots at recorded observation
steps. Reset route approach state only when the real driver would advance a
waypoint. Do not give the policy future events, raw expected anchors or the
simulator's state between samples.

Physical completion, command completion and policy observation are separate.
Keep pending generated deliveries and native driver ownership distinct from
the game input state. When the real simulation finishes, clear game input and
stop advancing physics. Drain already generated deliveries at their recorded
slots, maintaining driver ownership, but reject their keydowns as game input.
Only the terminal observation ends policy cycles and triggers the driver's
owned releases. Then generate and match the recorded results probe, `KeyW`
down followed by up, as protocol actions rather than another policy cycle.
Do not drop any of these records. The original browser helper also probes
Space and Escape; they are outside this recorder's movement domain and must
not be invented as recorded events or claimed covered by this replay.

The course medals tape is the required terminal overlap case: observation
287 at step 2,876 generates the transition whose `KeyD`, `KeyS` and `ShiftLeft`
deliveries are records 290 through 292 at step 2,894, after physical finish.
Their reset generation is 5 and default prevention is false. Terminal
observation 293 is followed by the three owned releases and the `KeyW` probe.
All three tapes contain the final probe.

Compare every intermediate anchor: position and velocity error at most 0.001,
wrapped angular error at most 0.00001 radians, elapsed error at most 0.001 ms,
dash energy error at most 0.00000001, and exact submerged state, course,
checkpoints, pearl counts, pearl IDs and race state. Rendered counts and browser
timestamps remain provenance, not simulated outputs. Report
the first disagreement with scenario, record/step and actual/expected values.
Do not widen tolerances to produce agreement. Always dispose the scene and
input ownership, including mismatch and construction failure paths.

Only after all intermediate states and generated transitions agree may the
three unchanged nonmedal results count as reproduced failures. Calibration
and mutation cases must expose a delivery shifted by one step, a missing or
wrong key edge, an altered observation, and a policy topology mismatch.
The independent calibration additionally covers an effectful single step
pulse, release/replacement at one completed step, an observation between
events at the same step, empty transitions and physical completion during a
pending transition. Fixed tolerances cannot be fitted to hosted residuals.
Resource ownership must return to zero. Synthetic calibration, scalar pitch
agreement, model fidelity and native qualification are distinct evidence.

The independent diagnostic reviewer found the terminal protocol and calibration
independence gaps above; both are now explicit. Its recorded anchor audit found
284 policy decisions consistent with captured release/press sets, which is
tape/policy consistency only, not evolving state reproduction. The corrected
design passed its informed recheck and is cleared for concrete planning.

### Baseline replay implementation plan

> Execute inline with the existing executing plans and TDD workflow. Keep
> physics fidelity, baseline reproduction and any future controller correction
> as separate gates. No application, native driver, asset or workflow changes.

**Goal:** persist the exact three timing tapes and reproduce their existing
failures through original physics and generated baseline control decisions.

**Architecture:** strict portable corpus loading precedes a single bounded
replay loop. Physical input, generated pending deliveries and driver ownership
are separate state. Integration tests provide an independent literal input
reference and run every recorded baseline. Use existing Zod, Vitest, original
asset loading, `InputController` and `SceneRuntime`; add no packages.

waypoint and keyboard decisions from the evolving simulation and matches

### Controller intervention selection

Baseline reproduction is complete, but the next controller must not inherit
its historical delivery slots as if changed commands had identical timing.
Two approaches remain within the native input boundary:

1. Keep continuous steering holds and replace endpoint orientation scoring
   with stateful interception prediction under explicitly modeled input delay.
   This changes less transport code, but steering duration still includes a
   slow observation round trip, which can make small corrections coarse.
2. Use bounded native steering pulses with interception prediction, retaining
   ordinary native throttle/brake control. Releasing steering before the next
   observation could narrow its effective duration. It changes the driver's
   command topology and therefore needs measured pulse timing and its own
   synthetic uncertainty model before qualification.

Investigate the second approach first; do not select it as a working controller
without evidence. The installed Playwright implementation performs one normal
`apiPress` input instrumentation pass, then native chord downs, an optional
real wall time delay, and native ups in reverse order. It accepts nonmodifier
chords. Internal `down`/`up` calls are not separate `apiDown`/`apiUp` calls.
This establishes API behavior, not physics hold duration or hosted timing.
Normal tracing options must remain enabled; fewer API boundaries are not a
license to disable snapshots, screenshots or network capture.

#### Bounded local pulse measurement design

This is a diagnostic input experiment, not controller implementation, an
earned progression scenario or a new hosted run. Use a temporary session
spec/config with the installed pinned Node and existing Playwright runner,
acceptance build and original game. Retain headless Chromium and existing
server lifecycle handling. Do not add a repository browser spec, workflow
switch, package, runtime hook or persistent machine setting.

Use one fresh context at 1024 by 768 and DPR 0.5. Through the actual UI disable
mouse steering, select reduced effects and load Sunlit. Reuse the existing
passive recorder, installing it only after ordinary canvas focus. Keep full
trace capture and all outcomes, drain once, then close the context.

Compare two explicitly different control strategies:

- Observed hold: existing batched native downs, one returned frame observation
  while held, then existing complete native release.
- Pulse: `page.keyboard.press(keys.join('+'), { delay: 100 })`, with no
  intervening observation while held. Register every potentially held key
  before the call, retain uncertainty on failure, and reuse complete release
  cleanup before propagating errors.

Run exactly five groups of four trials. Even groups use observed hold single
`d`, pulse `d+ArrowDown`, pulse single `a`, observed hold `a+ArrowUp`.
Odd groups reverse the strategy assignment, not the keys. Thus each strategy
has five single key and five two key trials, with 30 native downs and 30 ups
overall. The alternating groups only partially counterbalance individual
directions/chords, with a 3:2 split; retain group order and key identity in the
report. Do not describe this as a fully balanced causal comparison. All
movement remains genuine native input; never pause, change game time, inject
an input frame or reset game state during the attempt.

Take the same existing snapshot/frame observation between trials and stamp
it with the recorder. Record initial/final state, actual key event steps and
times, native trust/default prevention, frame dimensions and renderer class.
Keep machine specific details and raw trace files outside the public repo.
If play stops, visibility/focus changes, ownership/gating fails or expected
edges are missing, retain the partial evidence and report failure; do not
silently restart or substitute trials.

Predeclared data gate: exact trials/edges, trusted eligible playing keydowns,
ordered counters, no recorder interruption and complete cleanup. Predeclared
selection signal: every pulse key has at least one effective physics step,
maximum pulse hold is at most 12 physics steps, and pulse median hold is below
the observed hold median. A per key effective hold is
`keyup.steps - keydown.steps`; compute the selection median over these holds.
The two keys in a chord are correlated, not independent trials. Report
distributions by strategy, key count and key identity, including zero step
pulses and every outlier. Also report chord onset/release skew in steps and
recorded wall time. This compares the complete strategies, not isolated
transport speed. These are a local feasibility signal only, not a guarantee
of observation age, onset delay, hosted hold bounds or a medal result.

Use a 60 second active trial deadline, a 120 second total diagnostic bound
including setup/teardown, and no retry loop. The temporary runner configuration
must retain partial trace evidence on timeout; unavailable timing data is an
explicit failure, never a valid empty result. These bounds apply only to this
local diagnostic, not the original acceptance suite. Restore normal output
afterward and preserve the usual no hook boundary. Invalid or unavailable data
is inconclusive; a valid negative selection signal discards this pulse proposal
rather than relabeling the metric. If it passes, design the interception objective and an explicit
uncertainty model for the new topology; native hosted qualification remains
mandatory. The 100 ms hold is a native key duration, never a conversion to a
claimed fixed number of simulation steps.

The existing diagnostic Rubber Duck reviewer cleared this bounded experiment
in an informed followup, with the measurement units, partial counterbalance
and invalid data distinction above. This is one independent reviewer context,
not multiple reviewer consensus; the model was not changed.

existing servers -> fresh headless page -> UI settings/load/focus

#### Local pulse measurement evidence

**Baseline:** The existing continuous hold controller faithfully reproduces
all three hosted Sunlit failures. Its steering remains active across a full
returned observation.

**Hypothesis:** A fixed native 100 ms pulse can reduce median effective
steering duration locally while every key still affects at least one step
and no key exceeds twelve steps.

**Fixed acceptance:** Twenty declared trials, thirty downs and thirty ups,
valid native capture, complete cleanup; pulse minimum at least one step,
maximum at most twelve, median below observed hold. No metric changed.

The repaired diagnostic completed all twenty trials once, with retry zero,
in 11.522 seconds of test duration and 6.485 seconds of active measurement.
The complete reporter duration was 28.1 seconds, including both original
server builds. It reported no overall errors. The capture has 91 records:
31 anchors and 60 trusted native key events. It remained playing, visible
and focused, with no recorder failure, no reset and no owned keys left.
Completed physics advanced from step 78 to step 456.

| Strategy and key count  | Per key hold steps, in retained order                | Minimum | Median | Maximum |
| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------- | ------: | -----: | ------: |
| Observed hold, single   | 10, 11, 10, 9, 8                                     |       8 |     10 |      11 |
| Observed hold, pair     | 11, 11, 10, 10, 10, 10, 9, 9, 8, 8                   |       8 |     10 |      11 |
| Pulse, single           | 7, 6, 8, 7, 8                                        |       6 |      7 |       8 |
| Pulse, pair             | 11, 7, 12, 9, 11, 8, 10, 8, 10, 7                    |       7 |    9.5 |      12 |
| Observed hold, combined | 10, 11, 11, 10, 10, 11, 10, 10, 10, 9, 9, 9, 8, 8, 8 |       8 |     10 |      11 |
| Pulse, combined         | 11, 7, 7, 6, 12, 9, 11, 8, 8, 7, 10, 8, 10, 7, 8     |       6 |      8 |      12 |

There were no zero step pulses or pulse holds above twelve. The paired
pulses were not simultaneous: onset skew was one or two physics steps, and
release skew was also one or two steps. Recorded wall skew ranged from
20.6 to 29.2 ms for onset and 19.8 to 28.8 ms for release. The paired observed
holds had zero step skew, with at most 0.3 ms recorded wall skew.

Key identity also matters. Pulse `KeyD` holds were 11, 6, 11, 7, 10;
`KeyA` 7, 12, 8, 10, 8; `ArrowDown` 7, 8, 7; `ArrowUp` 9, 8.
Observed hold `KeyD` values were 10, 10, 10, 9, 8; `KeyA` 11, 11, 10, 9, 8;
`ArrowDown` 10, 9; `ArrowUp` 11, 10, 8. Group order and all per trial edge
steps/times remain in the attached report. Chord keys are correlated and
the five alternating groups remain only partially counterbalanced.

**Decision: keep for controller design only.** The declared local duration
signal passed, but the reduction is modest and paired pulse maxima are
larger than the observed hold maximum. This does not prove superiority,
predictable latency, a hosted timing bound or any medal outcome. In
particular the new model must preserve ordered chord onset/release rather
than treating both axes as simultaneous or mapping 100 ms to six steps.

The actual local surface was 491 by 336 drawing pixels at DPR 0.5, using a
software renderer. Full trace capture was retained on success as well as
the failed setup. Normal output was restored with ten JavaScript files and
zero acceptance global matches. The temporary production outputs were
removed; both reports and traces remain outside the public repository.

No application, controller, permanent test, dependency, asset, route, medal,
workflow, retry or existing timeout setting changed. No fifth hosted run
has started. The next action is a bounded Sunlit interception controller
design with explicit uncertainty for its changed native command topology,
followed by failing regressions before implementation.

The existing diagnostic Rubber Duck reviewer independently paired the raw
events and cleared this evidence for publication. It also inspected both full
