### Sunlit interception controller design

This is the next bounded candidate, not an approved implementation or a
working medal controller. Baseline replay remains unchanged characterization
of the old policy. The new driver must not reuse its historical command slots.

#### Intervention and scope

Prefer single axis pulses for the first controller candidate. The alternative,
paired pulses, can correct both axes in one call but had onset/release skew
and almost no median benefit locally. A single axis pulse has a smaller
action space and avoids that chord coupling, at the cost of separate
observations when both axes need correction. Continuous steering remains an
alternative if this candidate fails its real movement gates.

Each decision chooses one of ten native commands: cruise or Shift brake,
combined with no steering or one of `a`, `d`, `ArrowUp`, `ArrowDown` pressed
for 100 ms. Cruise leaves throttle neutral, so ordinary fish tuning approaches
speed six rather than accelerating toward twelve. This is a test driver
choice, not a gameplay speed change. No dash or W/S acceleration is needed
for this first candidate.

Keep the original waypoint positions, order, observed advancement and
checkpoint recovery helper. Predicting the next ordered waypoint as well as
the current one is permitted, but predicted contacts never advance the real
route or award a milestone. This matters for the pearls four units beyond
checkpoints: waiting until a delayed checkpoint observation to consider the
pearl can be too late.

Only the Sunlit browser driver changes. Kelpworks/Blacksmoker continue to use
the existing `courseKeyboardPolicy`, which also remains the baseline replay
policy. All game code, assets, race geometry, medal thresholds, browser
assertions, recording enrollment, deadlines and capture settings stay intact.

#### Pure prediction and explicit timing assumptions

Add `tests\fixtures\sunlitPulsePolicy.ts`. Inputs are a copied current fish
observation, previous observation step counter, currently owned brake state,
and the current/recovery target plus the next original waypoint. The policy
does not read recorder data, hidden simulation state, future samples, native
delivery slots, wall clocks or scheduled fixture delays.

The local single key timing measurements were yaw keys, not standalone pitch
pulses. Applying this transport hypothesis to pitch remains unqualified until
the new original physics and native evidence exist.

Use the real `stepFishMotion`, tuning and `CurrentVolume.sampleCurrent`,
including neutral pitch relaxation, velocity response and the authored warm
current. The predictor is not a collision simulator: actual original
`SceneRuntime` traversal remains the collision and award authority.

For each command, predict ordered native phases: old brake during observation
age; an optional native brake edge; pulse down; pulse up; neutral steering
while the new brake state persists. No brake change means no invented brake
operation; no pulse means no invented pulse operation. Own a possibly
delivered pulse key before `keyboard.press`, and remove it only after a
successful return. Existing full native release cleanup covers failures.

The first timing model uses three explicit synthetic scenarios, not measured
hosted bounds. Let `G` be the last observed step gap, initially 24 and clamped
to 6 through 90. Scenario total onset budgets are `0`, `ceil(G / 2)`, `G`;
their pulse holds are 6, 8 and 18 steps. Allocate each onset budget between
observation age and the actual command phases using the following exact
integer offsets from the sampled observation, whose completed step is zero.
For onset budget `D`, use age `A = floor(D / 3)`, command budget `C = D - A`,
and post action observation gap `O = max(1, ceil(G / 4))`.

| Actual operations      | Brake effect       | Pulse down | Pulse up | Next observation |
| ---------------------- | ------------------ | ---------- | -------- | ---------------- |
| Brake change and pulse | `A + floor(C / 2)` | `D`        | `D + H`  | `D + H + O`      |
| Brake change only      | `D`                | absent     | absent   | `D + O`          |
| Pulse only             | absent             | `D`        | `D + H`  | `D + H + O`      |
| Neither                | absent             | absent     | absent   | `A + O`          |

Here `H` is the scenario's pulse hold. An edge at completed step `k` first
affects step `k + 1`; preserve listed order when offsets coincide. Old brake
persists until its edge, then new brake persists. Steering is neutral except
between actual pulse down/up. For initial `G = 24`, the three scenarios with
both operations have `(age, brake, down, up, observation)` offsets
`(0, 0, 0, 6, 12)`, `(4, 8, 12, 20, 26)` and `(8, 16, 24, 42, 48)`.

These paired onset/hold choices are selected synthetic cases, not all
combinations or measured bounds. The later execution profiles must include
late/short and early/long combinations not used together in this predictor.
They are modeling assumptions, not a conversion of 100 ms into guaranteed
steps. After the next observation boundary, the remaining forecast tail
continues the new brake with neutral steering as a soft planning heuristic;
it is not a claim that the real controller cannot act again.

Bound every forecast to 240 physics steps from the observation, at 1/60.
There are ten candidates and three scenarios, so at most 7,200 motion steps
per decision. Reject nonfinite inputs or decreasing observation counters
explicitly. Do not silently replace invalid state with a successful command.

#### Interception objective

Score swept paths, not only final orientation. Use the existing movement and
contact geometry helpers for contact classification, requiring forward
checkpoint plane crossing and pearl contact with the actual combined radius.
Use distinct goal semantics rather than anonymous positions:

- Checkpoints use their actual award planes and radii. The last steering
  target remains z=93, but the actual terminal plane is z=92; finish requires
  no later observation at z=93.
- Pearls use their actual IDs and sphere contacts. Separately retain the
  waypoint depth and observed milestone requirements from the existing
  advancement helper. A predicted award is not observed advancement.
- Recovery is the original upstream approach with arrival within one unit at
  a predicted observation boundary. A sweep through that ball between samples
  does not complete recovery. Its next goal is the missed checkpoint, not the
  following pearl.

Track predicted awards in movement order, including fractional contact order
within a step. Normal lookahead geometry becomes eligible after the current
goal's required award and depth condition both hold along the predicted path.
Clip subsequent clearance/contact evaluation to that qualified interval, not
an earlier closest point. This is forecast eligibility only: actual waypoint
advancement still requires the real returned observation. Recovery eligibility
instead requires sampled arrival at the explicit next observation boundary.

For each of the first two goals, retain minimum clearance outside its radius
minus a 0.15 unit planning margin. Score squared clearance, adding
`16 * (1 + clearance)^2` when the path passes the goal plane without its
required contact before the next observation. A miss only in the heuristic
tail is a soft clearance cost, not an irreversible miss penalty. Weight the
current goal by one and the next by one quarter. A goal outside the horizon
still contributes its remaining clearance, not a constant failed-arrival
value. The margin belongs only to prediction, never to actual race awards.

Add a small arrival time cost (`0.003` per forecast step until both predicted
contacts or the horizon). At the next observation boundary, also score
progress/preparation toward the first unfinished goal:
`0.25 * distance^2 + 0.5 * (yawError^2 + pitchError^2) +
0.1 * velocityErrorSquared`. Desired world velocity points toward that goal
at speed `min(6, max(2, 2 * distance))`; subtract the sampled current to obtain
the desired body velocity. Derive desired yaw/pitch from that body direction.
This distinguishes useful pitch preparation and movement from a stationary,
yaw aligned brake hold. Omit this potential when the predicted chain is done.
Add `0.05` for a brake state change to avoid gratuitous native edges. Choose
the minimum worst scenario cost, with mean cost as the tie breaker and stable
candidate order for exact ties.

These fixed coefficients describe the first hypothesis, not acceptance
scores. Any later tuning is a separate recorded experiment; do not adjust
the real thirty second finish or milestone requirements.

#### Evidence gate before native wiring

Write and observe failing regressions before policy implementation. Cover
interception versus endpoint orientation, neutral pitch decay, current
drift, old brake during delayed delivery, no phantom operations, stopped
turning, stable/no conflicting keys, copied inputs, wrapped yaw, nonfinite
state and bounded work. In particular, a stationary braked fish aligned in
yaw but vertically offset must make useful preparation/progress without
depending on tie order; a second goal beyond the horizon must not cause a
stationary solution. Independent literal input sequences must calibrate the
new schedule before interpreting its traversal results.

Add a bounded fixture and integration file for newly generated command
timelines through original Sunlit and its original GLBs. Real movement must
award every checkpoint and pearl. The controller only receives copied
observations at the scheduled sample points. Pending native ownership must
still drain if physical finish precedes command completion.

Predeclare execution profiles in the concrete plan: prompt delivery, local
scale delay, sustained slower delay and an isolated long delay. They must
allocate a single total cycle budget over actual operations, preserve order,
and include startup coasting. Do not call them replays of the historical
corpus. Gate on all four checkpoints, all four actual pearl IDs, finish at
or below 30,000 ms, no injected awards/state resets and zero retained scene
ownership. Preserve every failed case and its timing profile.

Only then wire the native helper and cover pulse acknowledgement/cleanup,
brake ordering, terminal overlap and unchanged HUD/result assertions. Run one
existing complete earned scenario locally with full capture, restore normal
output, and pass the independent source gate before publication. A fifth
hosted run is justified only by reviewed changed source and these gates.
Task 14 still requires all 43 actual hosted cases and the subsequent main gate.

The first critique found missing pitch/progress incentives, conflated goal
semantics and ambiguous timing allocation. The revision above separates the
committed response interval from the soft continuation, adds preparation and
velocity potential, distinguishes sampled recovery from race awards, and
specifies all integer offsets. The same reviewer's recheck cleared the
candidate for concrete planning, not a new consensus vote. Preserve goal
completion at the next observation separately from later tail completion: a
later hypothetical contact must not suppress the earlier boundary potential.
This initial design did not establish liveness or medal performance. The
implementation and expanded actuator model are recorded below; native wiring
and qualification remain incomplete.

### Controller slice A: native pulse ownership implementation plan

**Goal:** Add the already measured public keyboard pulse operation to the
existing native ownership helper without wiring it into any controller yet.

**Architecture:** Extend `tests\fixtures\nativeKeyboard.ts`, sharing its
existing uncertain key set and release cleanup. The policy, generated
timelines and browser wiring are subsequent slices under the design above.
This small independent primitive is not a new native qualification result.

**Execution:** Inline with `executing-plans` and observed TDD, in the existing
feature checkout. Use the pinned runtime and existing Vitest/type/lint tools.

**Files:** `tests\fixtures\nativeKeyboard.ts`,
`tests\unit\nativeKeyboard.test.ts`, and this handoff.

- [x] Add six regressions to the existing unit file before the new export.
      Access the module namespace and assert the new function exists, so red
      is an assertion failure rather than a module resolution error. Reuse
      `deferred` and the existing typed keyboard mock, adding a typed `press`.
      The cases must establish the following exact behavior:

```ts
const input = keyboard();
const held = new Set(['Shift']);
const gate = deferred<void>();
input.press.mockReturnValue(gate.promise);
const operation = pulse()(input, held, 'a');
expect(input.press).toHaveBeenCalledWith('a', { delay: 100 });
expect(held).toEqual(new Set(['Shift', 'a']));
gate.resolve();
await operation;
expect(held).toEqual(new Set(['Shift']));
expect(input.up).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
```

Also reject `a+ArrowDown` and `Shift` without any native call or ownership
change, because the helper owns one steering key, not a chord or brake.
Reject an already owned pulse key without any native call; retain
both brake and pulse ownership on a rejected press acknowledgement;
and prove existing cleanup attempts every possible key while preserving
the original pulse error and a release error together. Each test uses
`pulse()` to assert and return `nativeKeyboard.pulseNativeKey`.

- [x] Run `npm run test -- tests\unit\nativeKeyboard.test.ts`. The six new
      cases must fail because `pulseNativeKey` is absent; the four original
      cases must remain green. Retain the red output.
- [x] Add this implementation, leaving existing helpers unchanged:

```ts
export async function pulseNativeKey<Key extends string>(
  keyboard: Pick<Page['keyboard'], 'press'>,
  held: Set<Key>,
  key: Key,
) {
  if (!['a', 'd', 'ArrowUp', 'ArrowDown'].includes(key))
    throw new Error('Native pulse requires one steering key.');
  if (held.has(key)) throw new Error('Native pulse key is already held.');
  held.add(key);
  await keyboard.press(key, { delay: 100 });
  held.delete(key);
}
```

- [x] Run the same unit selection and existing native consumers in one
      invocation:
      `npm run test -- tests\unit\nativeKeyboard.test.ts tests\unit\sunlitKeyboard.test.ts tests\unit\courseKeyboard.test.ts`.
      Run `npm run typecheck` and scoped ESLint/Prettier on changed files.
- [x] Record exact evidence, obtain the ordinary independent Rubber Duck
      source gate, and publish the slice. Do not dispatch hosted CI: the
      failing controller is still unchanged. The next concrete slice plans
      the explicit command timeline and goal semantics, including independent
      literal calibration, before implementing the interception objective.

#### Slice A evidence

All six new regressions failed at the missing function assertion before
implementation; the four existing cases passed. The implemented helper
accepts only one steering key, registers possible delivery before the native
100 ms press, and removes ownership only after success. Rejected
acknowledgements preserve ownership for the existing full release path.
Chord and brake inputs are rejected because treating either as one owned
steering key would make cleanup incorrect.

The combined existing runner selection passes eighteen cases: ten native
keyboard cases, six Sunlit driver cases and two other course driver cases.
Types and scoped lint pass. No consumer calls the new helper yet; this
establishes ownership and compatibility, not motion control or native
performance. The existing diagnostic Rubber Duck reviewer cleared the source
and its ownership coverage, in one informed context without additional
consensus. Implementation `c251510151bbc131d4e0bd9d3ffe28c2c44e70fb` is published.
The generated timeline and goal semantics implementation now exists in the
working tree, as recorded below.

### Controller implementation checkpoint: original physics gate passes

**Baseline:** Published `2ab0abf` contains the reviewed first candidate design
and points to the single steering ownership primitive. The native browser
driver still uses the old `courseKeyboardPolicy`.

**Hypothesis:** A generated native command timeline and interception objective,
with separate observation boundary and later continuation state, can earn the
original milestones under the declared delay profiles without changing race
rules, assets, browser assertions or deadlines.

**Published model files in `c0238a5`:** `tests\fixtures\sunlitPulsePolicy.ts`,
`tests\fixtures\simulateSunlitPulses.ts`,
`tests\unit\sunlitPulsePolicy.test.ts`, and
`tests\integration\sunlitPulseControl.test.ts`.

Fifteen observed missing timeline/goal regressions and nine missing predictor
regressions preceded implementation. The generated executor uses the original
three Sunlit GLBs, real `InputController` and original `SceneRuntime`. Its DOM
events remain synthetic and untrusted. An independent literal frame sequence
matches its full original runtime snapshot after 44 steps. Logical delivery
time continues after physical finish so pending delivery and cleanup drain.

The candidate expanded from ten commands to twenty five after the first
action set stalled. Each of five base modes combines with no steering or one
of A, D, Up and Down: cruise, held W acceleration, Shift brake, held S slowing,
or held S with a temporary W propulsion pulse. Real input coverage establishes
that W plus S means neutral throttle, and releasing W restores S deceleration.
Every pulse is still 100 ms. A propulsion chord presses W first, then optional
steering, and releases in reverse order. Explicit onset and release skew is
two steps by default. Paired steering is not in the candidate action set.

The timing contract no longer infers transport from observation cadence.
Its three fixed synthetic cases are onset/hold/observation 0/6/6, 36/12/12 and
90/18/23, plus the independently varied early long case 0/18/6. They are not
a cross product or measured hosted bounds. Each of 25 commands runs four
bounded forecasts. Planner truncation limits total motion work to 16,800
steps per decision, below the existing 18,000 limit; the public diagnostic
forecast still supports its full 240 step continuation. Original fish motion,
current volumes and contact geometry are reused, but collision geometry is
not predicted. Original physics remains the authority for acceptance.

Separate observed regressions repaired successor scoring before its
predecessor qualified, recovery scored at an unobserved continuation endpoint,
discontinuous stage potential, and checkpoint clearance incorrectly treated
as a sphere. Remaining route potential now uses boundary award state, true
disk projection and continuous depth residuals. A sphere contact after its
center depth incorrectly incurred a 43.56 miss penalty. The new regression
observed that exact error. The correction checks the sphere's far extent,
not its center plane; checkpoint risk still uses the actual disk plane.
Removing pearl miss risk entirely was tried and discarded after four failures.

The planner ranks predicted miss risk first, followed by maximum
scenario relative regret and mean regret. It compares lost opportunity
within each timing case rather than raw values at different physical times.
The guard accounts for an independently delayed next correction. This is
still a conservative selected timing model, not proof of hosted bounds.

The same independent reviewer identified three temporal defects.
Unequal observation times rewarded an unnecessary opening turn; an empty
command reduced the inferred delay from sixty to twenty four steps; and an
unseen depth crossing could promote a waypoint even after the fish returned
upstream before observation. Observed regressions preceded each correction.
The common valuation clock is now `D + H + 2 * skew + O`, without changing
actual observation delivery. Actual boundary state remains separate.
Returned depth is evaluated at that sample, retaining only genuine earlier
observed progress.

Observed regressions also exposed obligations that appeared only after a
pearl was earned: heading cost increased from zero to 0.082925, velocity cost
from 0.4 to 3.6, and geometric/arrival cost from 118.09254 to 119.07716 at an
otherwise identical stopped pose. The candidate anticipates following
orientation and velocity obligations with ordered path lengths. An additional
review found that replacing the pearl center with a new depth projection
still increased spatial cost for an offset pose. The implemented correction
keeps the existing spatial vertex as an optional route choice after earning,
including across cursor advancement. An ordered graph minimizes additive
squared route leg energy. Previously feasible routes stay available when an
award removes a requirement. Six offset, wrapped angle and nonzero current
cases explicitly check distance, heading, velocity and whole score
monotonicity; an exhaustive subset oracle checks the optimizer.

A separate regression showed that advancing the prediction window alone
raised terminal distance value from 3.8025 to 147.8726, despite identical fish,
awards and remaining task. The final objective has full score window
invariance, canonical obligations and no credit for contacts in an
uncontrolled future continuation. Clearance remains directly observable for
geometry assertions; it is no longer hidden inside a separate speculative
tail score. Representation dependent assertions now check the actual route
energy and clearance terms rather than a vacuous subtraction.

**Acceptance:** Every profile must finish at or below 30,000 ms with all four
checkpoints in order, every actual pearl ID, original assets and zero retained
input, scene or asset ownership. The forty second cap is diagnostic, not an
expanded acceptance deadline. The fixed profiles were declared before running

### Fifth hosted diagnosis and startup correction

The retained traces identify work performed after the race starts but before
the first controller observation. Hover and focus consumed 3,767.8 ms in Course
Medals and 2,597.0 ms in Blacksmoker. Course Medals first observed step 383
after checkpoint two was already missed. Blacksmoker first observed step 285,
but its first S keydown arrived at step 382. Gaps around policy evaluation
also contribute, but trace gaps are not pure CPU measurements.

Recorded native event replay reproduced the Blacksmoker and Kelpworks finishes
at 53,091.504978 and 26,986.350473 ms. Every replayed observation had zero
position error. Course Medals was replayed only through the existing bounded
prefix ending at step 7,198, not through its entire capture. Recorded replay
establishes fidelity for those events, not qualification of changed commands.

Source `69884c8` contains the correction and has local native evidence:

- `tests\browser\acceptance-helpers.ts` prepares the pure planner, parks the
  pointer and focuses the launch button before native Enter starts the race.
  Playing, canvas count and focus assertions remain, as does passive recorder
  enrollment. Ordinary `selectSunlit` click behavior is unchanged.
- `tests\fixtures\sunlitPulsePolicy.ts` caches the validated uniform current
  field from an authoritative sample and inclusive authored bounds. This
  removes 17,000 repeated current validations per decision without changing
  candidate motion. Pure planner preparation is idempotent and never delivers
  its synthetic state or generated command to the game.
- The driver reports initial steps plus first, maximum and total planner wall
  time, including failure exits, without additional browser round trips.
- `tests\unit\sunlitStartup.test.ts` covers ordering and failure before launch.
  The existing policy, keyboard and original physics test files add current
  boundary equivalence, bounded work, timing evidence and startup
  characterization.

Two startup regressions failed against the previous ordering. The current
work regression observed 17,000 validations against an allowance of one;
eighteen authoritative face boundary cases already passed before the
optimization. A separate regression observed missing planner timing evidence.
The red logs are `fifth-startup-red.log`, `fifth-current-work-red.log` and
`fifth-planner-observability-red.log`.

Source correction `69884c8` and its checkpoint `3875ba6` are published.

independent, original physics, complete native and full local gates, not an

**Actual result:** All 43 cases ran once with expected status `passed`.
Forty one passed and two failed, with no retries, skips, flaky results or
overall reporter errors. All three normal production cases passed. Baseline
passed 1,963 Vitest cases in 61 files and fifteen bootstrap checks. Both failures
were unchanged nonnull medal assertions, at `blacksmoker.spec.ts:222` and
`course-medals.spec.ts:45`. Both Sunlit races physically finished with every

### Sixth hosted timing diagnosis

Scalar steering onset and hold measurements alone do not describe a propulsion
chord. Course Medals had steering onsets of 12 through 63 steps and holds of
six through seventeen steps, yet still failed. Its first observed forward
passage pearl miss has the following preceding interval:

| Event                                                       | Physics step |
| ----------------------------------------------------------- | -----------: |
| Observation near `[-4.011, -4.467, 62.626]`                 |          893 |
| W down with S retained                                      |          923 |
| Down arrow down                                             |          934 |
| Down arrow up                                               |          944 |
| W up                                                        |          949 |
| Observation near `[-5.089, -4.881, 64.546]`, pearl absent   |          964 |
| Observation beyond the pearl's forward extent, still absent |        1,069 |

W was held for 26 steps while steering lasted ten. The leading and trailing
offsets were eleven and five steps, not the model's default symmetric two
steps. Across this capture the largest leading offset was eleven and trailing
offset seven; Blacksmoker observed fifteen and seven, and passing Kelpworks
five and six. These are observed samples, not transport bounds. The planner's
cost, native edge scheduling and their causal contributions must be separated
before claiming an optimization is sufficient.

Course Medals was first observed with the passage pearl at 26.950 seconds and
home at 40.067 seconds. Blacksmoker was first observed with passage at 22.100
seconds and home at 80.783 seconds. These anchors do not establish exact pickup
times. No Blacksmoker anchor was beyond the home pearl's forward
extent without that award, so this is not labeled a proven ordinary forward
miss. Its stalled approach and recovery require separate attribution.

| Experiment field  | Current record                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| ----------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline          | Earlier startup is observed, but the sixth run still passed only 41 of 43 cases. Actual planner wall cost and asymmetric chord offsets exceed assumptions used to infer local sufficiency.                                                                                                            |
| Hypothesis        | Repeated forecast computation and allocation materially age observations and may affect delivery scheduling. Profile their contribution rather than assuming wall time is all CPU or retuning trajectories.                                                                                           |
| Acceptance metric | Demonstrate a material measured reduction with observed failing work regressions and exact prediction equivalence, preserve every existing profile and native assertion, and independently review any timing or architecture change. All 43 hosted cases and the automatic main gate remain required. |
| Result            | The controller implementer owns measured computation diagnosis. No new correction or seventh hosted attempt is claimed.                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Decision          | Keep the evidenced startup repair, but the sixth run did not complete hosted qualification. No unchanged rerun, larger deadline, weaker medal rule, hidden race pause, removed tracing or speculative weight change.                                                                                  |

The completed monitor is no longer running. Its log is
`14-hosted-sixth-watch.log`; complete job output is
`14-hosted-sixth-complete-job.log`. Remote main remains
`74399a665cf8e1f074d158bbac3e87a801887b7f`, an ancestor of the candidate.
No integration or deployment has occurred.

**Next action:** Profile the dominant controller hot paths and allocations.
An exact optimization requires observed regressions and oracle equivalence.
If the remedy changes timing coverage, candidate topology or control
architecture, review a concrete evidenced plan before implementation.
Do not substitute another local pass for the hosted gate. Task 15 remains
after Task 14.

### Sixth hosted computation experiment

The isolated V8 profile attributes 25.0% of baseline CPU samples to
`pickupIntersection` and 23.2% to `predictPulse`. Iterator allocations account
for 14.8% of estimated allocation. This identifies local computational work,
not hosted CPU contention or the cause of each delayed native edge.

The two file correction `61f673a` has independent source clearance:
`tests\fixtures\sunlitPulsePolicy.ts` and
`tests\unit\sunlitPulsePolicy.test.ts`.
Strictly disjoint movement and pearl bounding boxes avoid unnecessary
intersection solving; boundary and extreme coordinate cases retain the
authoritative predicate and error path. An ordered active goal cursor replaces
the per tick iterator. Timing, weights, candidates, pulse duration, motion
integration, objectives and the current field are unchanged.

Two observed failing regressions measured 240 unnecessary pickup calls and
244 iterator calls against an allowance of three. Twenty boundary and overflow
characterizations already passed before implementation. All 22 cases now pass.
The parent reproduced all 123 affected cases, including the previous 101,
with exactly unchanged finish times for all five immutable physics profiles.

The retained benchmark reconstructs 152 decision inputs from all three sixth
run captures. Five repetitions produce 760 measured calls. This is a
computation experiment, not replay of changed native commands.

| Metric                              |      Baseline |                   Optimized |
| ----------------------------------- | ------------: | --------------------------: |
| Total wall ms, 760 calls            |    16,171.413 |                   9,417.892 |
| Summed process CPU ms, 760 calls    |        16,125 |                       9,390 |
| Median wall ms                      |        23.201 |                      12.288 |
| p95 wall ms                         |        24.859 |                      13.680 |
| Sampled allocation bytes, 152 calls | 8,831,612,552 | Approximately 4.330 billion |

Wall and CPU totals decreased 41.8%; sampled allocation decreased 51.0%.
Allocation is estimated churn, not resident memory. All 152 selected decisions
and 15,200 complete forecasts match their saved baseline exactly, including
cost, contact, state and clearance outputs. These finite comparisons support
equivalence on the recorded corpus, not a universal proof.

A separate experiment used fresh Node processes and unforced GC observation:

| Metric, 760 calls   | Baseline | Optimized |
| ------------------- | -------: | --------: |
| Wall ms             |   16,015 |     9,663 |
| Process CPU ms      |   16,172 |     9,813 |
| GC event count      |    1,721 |     1,102 |
| Summed GC event ms  |   356.62 |    220.14 |
| Maximum GC event ms |    1.080 |     1.162 |

This supports reduced local computation and collection work. It does not show
a reduction in maximum GC delay or establish any causal link between GC and
the hosted eleven/five step chord offsets. Use the separate process
`sixth-chord-gc-diagnostic-baseline` and `sixth-chord-gc-diagnostic-after`
artifacts, not the earlier exploratory comparison in one process.

| Experiment field  | Current record                                                                                                                                                 |
| ----------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline          | The isolated profile identifies unnecessary intersection work and repeated iterator allocation in the expensive forecast loop.                                 |
| Hypothesis        | Reject provably disjoint movement boxes and advance an ordered active goal cursor without changing prediction semantics.                                       |
| Acceptance metric | At least 35% reduction in measured wall and CPU totals, at least 40% reduction in sampled allocation, exact corpus outputs and all existing profile gates.     |
| Result            | The predeclared local metric and independent source gate pass. The asymmetric native timing family remains unresolved.                                         |
| Decision          | Keep as a computational optimization only. Do not dispatch another hosted run on computation improvement alone or claim a portable latency or medal guarantee. |

Evidence includes `sixth-geometry-red.log`, `sixth-geometry-green.log`,

The parent's focused output is `14-parent-computation-gate.log`.
Parent types, scoped lint and formatting pass, with output in

The same independent Rubber Duck reviewer found no blocking source findings,

### Asymmetric chord representation implementation plan

**Review status:** Implemented at `a1f1157` after readiness clearance and
observed TDD. The independent implementation gate clears publication of this
bounded representation, prefix and replay milestone, not selector changes
or qualification.

One fresh independent Rubber Duck context reviewed this plan, with
`claude-opus-5` and maximum effort requested. This is one review context,
not consensus ranking. The revised draft adds the predictor negative control,
derived route state, a specific replay mismatch assertion, explicit retained S
verification and source level prefix derivation.
The suggested `body === undefined` type error was not reproduced: the installed
TypeScript compiler reported no diagnostics under strict checking. The generic
prefix ownership is already bounded by eight enumerated codes, duplicate
ownership rejection and the existing record limit; no arbitrary smaller cap
is added. The work ceiling is clarified as prediction work, not an invented
replay wall time budget. An informed recheck uses the existing reviewer
context; it is not another independent consensus vote. That recheck confirmed
readiness and independently verified the hashes, S ownership, strict compiler
result and existing validation bounds. Its tuple formatting correction is
applied below. The completed implementation gate is recorded after the plan.

> **For agentic workers:** Use `subagent-driven-development` or
> `executing-plans` after the readiness gate. Preserve observed TDD and publish
> only after the independent source gate.

**Goal:** Represent the recorded eleven step leading and five step trailing
offsets, reproduce their original runtime context, and preserve default
controller behavior.

**Architecture:** Add an optional release offset while retaining the existing
leading offset and its default. Reuse the strict timing corpus validator and
original replay executor through an explicitly incomplete prefix API. A
different symmetric constant cannot reproduce the four recorded edges. A
general operation scheduler would enlarge scope without being necessary for
this interval, so it is not selected.

**Stack:** Existing TypeScript, Vitest, Node crypto/filesystem APIs,
`InputController`, `SceneRuntime` and original GLBs. No new dependency.

**Non goals:** No selector changes, new timing scenarios, native driver
changes, changed thresholds, native trial or hosted qualification. Scenario
coverage and its cost remain a separate subsequent gate.

```ts
const recordedChord = {
  brakeHeld: false,
  slowing: true,
  propel: true,
  pulse: 'ArrowDown',
} as const;

  onsetSteps: 30,

  skewSteps: 11,
  releaseSkewSteps: 5,
};

it('represents the asymmetric recorded chord with S retained', () => {
  expect(
    control.sunlitPulseTimeline(false, recordedChord, asymmetricTiming, true),
  ).toEqual({
    age: 10,
    events: [
      { at: 30, key: 'w', type: 'keydown' },
      { at: 41, key: 'ArrowDown', type: 'keydown' },
      { at: 51, key: 'ArrowDown', type: 'keyup' },
      { at: 56, key: 'w', type: 'keyup' },
    ],
    observeAt: 71,
  });
});

it('uses asymmetric completion for valuation and risk truncation', () => {
  const p = control.predictSunlitPulse(
    { ...observation(), slowing: true },

    asymmetricTiming,

  expect(p.boundaryFish).toEqual(p.evaluationFish);

    control.sunlitPulseTimeline(
      false,
      { brakeHeld: false, slowing: true, pulse: null },
      asymmetricTiming,
      true,
    ),
  ).toEqual({ age: 10, events: [], observeAt: 25 });
});
```

```ts
function pulseSkews(timing: SunlitPulseTiming) {
  const press = timing.skewSteps ?? 2;
  const release =
    timing.releaseSkewSteps === undefined ? press : timing.releaseSkewSteps;
  counter(press);
  counter(release);
  return { press, release };
}
```

plus observation delay when there are no operations. Keep

- [x] Implement `sunlitChordWitness.ts` with the provenance above and:

```ts
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { readFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';
import { parseNativeTimingPrefix } from './nativeTimingCorpus';

export function parseSunlitChordWitness(input: unknown) {
  const body = JSON.stringify(input);
  if (
    body === undefined ||
    createHash('sha256').update(body).digest('hex') !==
      SUNLIT_CHORD_PROVENANCE.prefixSha256
  ) {
    throw new Error('Sunlit chord prefix checksum mismatch.');
  }
  return parseNativeTimingPrefix(input);
}

export async function loadSunlitChordWitness() {
  return parseSunlitChordWitness(
    JSON.parse(
      await readFile(
        resolve(
          'tests',
          'fixtures',
          'native-timing',
          'sunlit-34013534603-prefix-964.json',
        ),
        'utf8',
      ),
    ),
  );
}
```

and checksum rejection after changing a timestamp. Filesystem, JSON, checksum
and validation errors propagate without fallback evidence.

```ts
expect([...held]).toEqual(['KeyS']);
```

```ts
for (const key of ['yaw', 'pitch', 'roll'] as const) {
  const delta = forecast.boundaryFish[key] - after.snapshot.fish[key];
  expect(
    Math.abs(Math.atan2(Math.sin(delta), Math.cos(delta))),
  ).toBeLessThanOrEqual(0.00001);
}
expect(
  Math.abs(forecast.boundaryFish.dashEnergy - after.snapshot.fish.dashEnergy),
).toBeLessThanOrEqual(0.00000001);
expect(forecast.boundaryFish.isSubmerged).toBe(after.snapshot.fish.isSubmerged);
```

This is a prediction test using a replayed observation, not runtime seeding.

### Additional retained timing sample for the following gate

The later Blacksmoker recovery interval runs from observation 1,970 to 2,064,
with W down 2,013, Up down 2,025, Up up 2,054 and W up 2,059. Its relative
offsets are 43/55/84/89 with observation at 94. Leading offset, steering hold
and trailing offset are twelve, twenty nine and five steps.
The event clock records 201.3 ms, 485.0 ms and 68.1 ms respectively, for
754.4 ms of W ownership. Thus this long physical hold also has substantial
wall duration; it is not explained merely by labeling physics ticks as time.

The fish moves from approximately `[0.924, -4.902, 84.444]` to
`[-2.098, -3.469, 84.142]` without the home pearl. Its observed distance to that
pearl increases from 1.365 to 2.169. This is after the thirty second medal
deadline and is not claimed to be the earliest cause of the missed medal.
It is a retained sample for the subsequent coverage decision, not an adopted
scenario or a native timing bound. No additional replay or native execution
was used to characterize it.

### Bounded scenario coverage and computation design

**Status:** Evaluator readiness cleared after two acceptance requirements
were made explicit. The first matched cost experiment failed. The subsequent
compact storage correction passes its renewed source gate and fixed v2 cost
experiment, as recorded below. No changed selector, native browser run or
seventh hosted attempt has been executed.

**Review path:** Rubber Duck, one independent reviewer context using the
existing reviewer with its model unchanged. No three context consensus is
claimed. The review accepted the prefix key, exclusive ownership schedule,
portable reference and distinct work counters, while requiring complete
four case matrix comparisons and errors during both fork and transfer
advancement. Both requirements are included below. It permits evaluator
implementation and the declared conditional cost experiment only.

**Goal:** Add the demonstrated Course 893 timing interaction without removing
existing coverage or exceeding 18,000 actual motion calls per decision.

**Hypothesis:** The existing selector overlooks a physically unsafe command
because all four forecasts omit the recorded chord phase. Evaluating that
phase alongside the existing cases can reject it when a safe candidate exists.
Exact shared prefix evaluation may make the additional case affordable, but
its CPU and allocation effects must be measured before changing selection.

**Architecture under review:** Compile deterministic input and recovery
observation prefixes before the race. Evaluate shared prediction transitions
once per observation, then retain the existing candidate ordering and score
selection. Keep independent physical state for each new observation and
preserve the public diagnostic predictor.

**Stack:** Existing TypeScript, Vitest, Node measurement tools and original
runtime fixtures. No dependencies, application changes or runtime overrides.

#### Evidence and alternatives

For a chord, D is observation to W down, H is steering hold, L is W down to
steering down, R is steering up to W up, and O is final release to observation.
The table describes selected cases, not bounds on transport.

| Case                   | D/H/O    | L/R  | W hold | Planning steps |
| ---------------------- | -------- | ---- | -----: | -------------: |
| P, existing prompt     | 0/6/6    | 2/2  |     10 |            124 |
| L, existing middle     | 36/12/12 | 2/2  |     16 |            172 |
| Z, existing late       | 90/18/23 | 2/2  |     22 |            240 |
| E, existing early long | 0/18/6   | 2/2  |     22 |            136 |
| C, Course 893          | 30/10/15 | 11/5 |     26 |            179 |
| B, Blacksmoker 1970    | 43/29/5  | 12/5 |     46 |            202 |

At the replayed Course 893 state, the unchanged selected command has zero
miss penalty in P/L/Z/E and `25.339445966339053` in C. The existing S candidate
without a pulse has zero across all five. The retained failing diagnostic
asserts zero actual C miss penalty for the selected command and observes the
nonzero result. This supplements, rather than replaces, the pinned physical
replay and equal duration negative control.

The Course D/L/H/R/O event clock durations are
538.3/192.7/158.8/80.1/212.3 ms. Blacksmoker's are
739.6/201.3/485.0/68.1/66.9 ms. These are not planner CPU measurements.
The planner stopwatch surrounds computation before native delivery, whereas
D also includes transport and other intervening work.

Twenty five of 35 retained two key chords whose endpoints were still running
before thirty seconds were asymmetric, including chords from the passing
Kelpworks consumer. Asymmetry alone is not a failure classifier.
The Blacksmoker 1970 forecast matches its endpoint within approximately
0.00001210 position distance but has no forward miss penalty. Blacksmoker
605 also contains a nineteen step single key steering hold. Base transitions
and observation gaps remain additional modeling limitations.

| Alternative | Uncached steps | Decision                                                            |
| ----------- | -------------: | ------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| P/L/Z/E/C   |         21,275 | Recommended target, conditional on exact computation and cost gates |
| P/L/C/B     |         16,925 | Reject: removes late Z and previously necessary early long E        |
| P/L/Z/E/C/B |         26,325 | Reject for this design: shared prefix work still exceeds 18,000     |

The six case option needs 20,221 to 20,960 motion only prefixes, or 20,697 to
20,973 prefixes when recovery observations are included. This rejects the
investigated method, not every conceivable future algorithm.
The recommended set retains every old case and adds only C. It does not
cover every observed leading offset, the nineteen step single key hold,
the later twenty nine step hold, all base transition phases or arbitrary
correlations. Neither observed maxima nor this finite set are timing bounds.

#### Work and cost baseline

For P/L/Z/E/C, the symbolic prefix counts include the recovery observation
branch and all eight currently accepted held base combinations:

| Held base      | Prefix transitions |
| -------------- | -----------------: |
| None           |             16,958 |
| W              |             16,964 |
| S              |             16,924 |
| W and S        |             16,859 |
| Shift          |             16,964 |
| Shift and W    |             16,981 |
| Shift and S    |             16,981 |
| Shift, W and S |             16,889 |

These are symbolic counts, not an implemented evaluator's measured work.
The current policy still performs 16,800 motion calls.
Across 152 retained inputs and three repetitions, that unchanged policy
measured median 12.727 ms, p95 14.723 ms and 5.968 seconds aggregate process
CPU across 456 calls. A five case batch of public diagnostic forecasts
measured median 21.509 ms but performed 30,000 ticks because those forecasts
retain their 240 tick tails. It is not a planner cost measurement.

The integer only prefix index experiment measured median 1.200 ms and
approximately 1.84 MB sampled allocation per build, before prediction states
are stored. Its map must not be rebuilt every decision. Actual evaluator
wall time, process CPU, retained heap and GC effects remain unmeasured.

##### A. Interface and bounded compilation

```ts
export type SunlitPulseForecast = ReturnType<typeof predictSunlitPulse>;
export type SunlitPulseWork = Readonly<{
  integrations: number;
  expandedTicks: number;
  propagationStateAllocations: number;
  peakOwnedStates: number;
  stateIndexSlots: number;
  finalLiveStates: number;
}>;
export interface SunlitPulseMatrix {
  readonly candidates: readonly {
    readonly command: SunlitPulseCommand;
    readonly forecasts: readonly SunlitPulseForecast[];
  }[];
  readonly work: SunlitPulseWork;
}
export interface CompiledSunlitPulseEvaluator {
  readonly bounds: readonly (SunlitPulseWork & { readonly mask: number })[];
  evaluate(observed: SunlitPulseObservation): SunlitPulseMatrix;
}
export function compileSunlitPulseEvaluator(
  timings: readonly SunlitPulseTiming[],
): CompiledSunlitPulseEvaluator;
```

`bounds` describes immutable symbolic predictions for masks zero through
seven. Mask bits are Shift four, S two and W one. `work` records execution.
Each forecast retains all seventeen existing fields, including terminal,
boundary and evaluation fish, contacts, clearance, miss penalties and potential
terms. Forecast `motionSteps` remains its logical horizon. Policy
`motionSteps` remains the original 16,800 expanded ticks, preserving existing
decision objects. Actual integrations are counted and exposed separately,
not hidden by changing the meaning of either quantity.

The topology compiler takes paths containing `symbols`, `observeAt` and
`evaluationAt`, and emits immutable operations:

```ts
type Operation =
  | {
      kind: 'advance';
      node: number;
      parent: number;
      depth: number;
      symbol: number;
      takeParent: boolean;
      retire: readonly number[];
    }
  | {
      kind: 'finish';
      row: number;
      leaf: number;
      boundary: number;
      evaluation: number;
      retire: readonly number[];
    };
```

- [x] Generate paths in existing candidate then scenario order. Apply events
      at the same tick in existing order before integrating that tick.
      Preserve logical W/S ownership while generating future physical frames.
- [x] Encode the complete physical frame and returned observation bit:

```ts
const frame =
  ((Number(brakeHeld) * 3 + throttle + 1) * 3 + steerX + 1) * 3 + steerY + 1;
const symbol = frame * 2 + Number(completed === observeAt);
const prefixKey = parentId * 108 + symbol;
```

Brake and throttle stay separate. All other physical frame fields are
constant for these commands. Prefix identity includes its parent, so depth
and exact prior history are retained. Never merge approximately equal fish.

- [x] Insert a node only at the first encounter of its prefix, retaining its
      actual depth. A node ID is not elapsed time. Append `finish` immediately
      after each forecast, preserving scalar finalization and error order.
- [x] Calculate final reads from child advances and each finish's leaf,
      boundary and evaluation reads. Transfer a parent only at its final
      reader. Retire other nodes immediately after their final reader.
- [x] Reject creation of integration node 18,001. Compile every held mask
      before returning an evaluator. Discard construction maps and temporary
      paths. Execution uses indexed operations, retirement lists and
      predecoded immutable input frames, not new maps.
- [x] Prepare the fixed four case singleton through
      `prepareSunlitPulsePolicy()`. Direct policy calls lazily obtain that
      singleton after the existing history validation. Explicitly compiled
      evaluators are caller owned, not a growing global cache.

The key suffices only because propagation depends on the identical root,
prior state, physical input, tick depth and recovery observation branch.
Every private planning tick is within its intervention cutoff, so risk is
active throughout the truncated path. Final scoring and sampling remain
specific to each forecast. The public 240 tick diagnostic tail retains its
separate risk cutoff condition and does not use this truncated topology.

##### B. Exclusive state ownership

```ts
interface PropagationState {
  fish: FishState;
  awarded: boolean[];
  awardAt: number[];
  contacts: Array<number | null>;
  clearance: number[];
  irreversibleMiss: number[];
  activeGoal: number;
}
function forkState(parent: PropagationState): PropagationState {
  return {
    ...parent,
    awarded: parent.awarded.slice(),
    awardAt: parent.awardAt.slice(),
    contacts: parent.contacts.slice(),
    clearance: parent.clearance.slice(),
    irreversibleMiss: parent.irreversibleMiss.slice(),
  };
}
```

Context contains immutable goals, initial qualification count, waypoint
and validated held flags, but no state links.

- [x] Extract the existing arithmetic into private `advanceOwned(...)`,
      mutating only an exclusively owned state. Preserve operation order,
      current sampling, pickup predicates and authoritative contact helpers.
      Copy all five arrays at branches. Fish can initially be borrowed
      because motion returns a new fish and must not mutate input vectors.
- [x] On the parent's final read, remove its arena entry and transfer the
      same state to the child. Exclude that consumed entry from ordinary
      retirement. Every evaluation creates a fresh state index.
- [x] Finalize immediately using that forecast's leaf history and exact
      boundary/evaluation samples. Freeze output copies, never arena arrays.
      Detach output fish/vectors and arrays from other rows. Preserve fish
      aliases within a forecast when sampled states coincide.
- [x] Clear all arena entries in `finally`, including on errors. Never
      retain prediction state in the compiled evaluator or across observations.

The symbolic ownership schedule has 346 branch copies, 347 propagation
state allocations and at most 29 simultaneously owned states for five cases.
With at most nine entries per goal array, that is 1,730 branch array copies
and at most 15,570 copied elements, plus root initialization. This excludes
fish, geometry helper and output allocations. It is not a heap measurement.

| Mask | Four case integrations | Five case integrations | Five case peak owned states |
| ---: | ---------------------: | ---------------------: | --------------------------: |
|    0 |                 13,240 |                 16,958 |                          17 |
|    1 |                 13,253 |                 16,964 |                          20 |
|    2 |                 13,224 |                 16,924 |                          29 |
|    3 |                 13,164 |                 16,859 |                          20 |
|    4 |                 13,253 |                 16,964 |                          23 |
|    5 |                 13,263 |                 16,981 |                          22 |
|    6 |                 13,263 |                 16,981 |                          20 |
|    7 |                 13,191 |                 16,889 |                          19 |

The initial node indexed implementation used up to 16,982 state references,
distinct from live state count. The compact correction below replaces that
storage layout while retaining the same logical prefixes.
The warmup test's first invocation becomes 13,240 actual motion calls;
the second invocation must still add none. Existing gameplay assertions stay.

##### C. Error contract and observed regressions

Compilation validates an array of one through five timings, then each timing
in index order. D and H are nonnegative counters; O is at least one. Retain
press/release defaults and counter, completion and horizon validation order.
Validate a canonical cruise timeline and common valuation before all masks.
Nonarray/nonobject containers throw `TypeError`; empty/oversized arrays throw
`RangeError`. Work overflow throws
`RangeError('Pulse evaluator exceeds 18000 motion calls.')`.

Evaluation preserves the scalar first forecast validation order: steps,
finite fish, timeline/held flags, horizon, previous history, goals and root.
Policy retains its earlier history check. No fallback, clamping or partial
successful matrix is allowed.

- [x] Write regressions and observe their failures before implementation.
      Missing API is the initial feature failure; separately measure the
      frozen scalar reference's 21,275 integrations for five cases to establish
      the actual work that must disappear.
- [x] Spy directly on `stepFishMotion`. Compilation must call it zero times.
      Evaluation must match the table for every held mask, independently of
      reported counters. Restore the spy before running the reference.
- [x] Exercise duplicate paths and distinct recovery observations:

```ts
const root: SunlitPulseObservation = {
  fish: {
    position: [0, -4, 0],
    velocity: [0, 0, 0],
    yaw: 0,
    pitch: 0,
    roll: 0,
    dashEnergy: 1,
    isSubmerged: true,
  },
  steps: 0,
  waypoint: 0,
  checkpointIndex: 0,
  collectedPearlIds: [],
  approachingCheckpoint: false,
  brakeHeld: false,
};
const recovery = {
  ...root,
  approachingCheckpoint: true,
  fish: { ...root.fish, position: [0, -4, 4.5], velocity: [0, 0, 6] },
} satisfies SunlitPulseObservation;
const early = { onsetSteps: 0, holdSteps: 6, observationSteps: 10 };
const late = { ...early, observationSteps: 30 };
const matrix = compileSunlitPulseEvaluator([early, late, early]).evaluate(
  recovery,
);
expect(matrix.candidates[0].forecasts.map((f) => f.contacts[0])).toEqual([
  10,
  null,
  10,
]);
```

The root is outside the recovery sphere to avoid vacuous qualification.
Deep freeze inputs, compare complete reference rows, prove output ownership
and prove another evaluation cannot alter earlier results.

- [x] Compile C followed by P and use root x equal to `1e155`. A motion spy
      delegates normally but throws a sentinel for nonzero `steerX`.

- [x] Separately inject the original sentinel error during a known fork

- [x] D/H/both skews zero with O one must retain ordered zero time edges,

- [x] Cover negative, fractional, NaN, infinite and unsafe counters, O zero

- [x] Five timings with D `[0, 3, 6, 9, 12]`, H 200 and O one must fail the

| Measurement                                      | Frozen four case A | Five case matrix B |
| ------------------------------------------------ | -----------------: | -----------------: |
| Sampled allocated bytes                          |     21,722,090,448 |     20,505,409,408 |
| GC events                                        |              1,075 |                253 |
| Total observed GC ms                             |            217.509 |            395.072 |

| Maximum GC ms                                    |             1.0925 |             5.1247 |

A's observed events are all minor collections. B has 181 minor, 36 major
and 36 incremental events, using the pinned runtime's kind constants.
The allocator samples include objects collected by both minor and major GC;
compare this pair, not earlier profiles with potentially different sampling
settings. Sampled churn is not resident memory.

Thus modestly lower sampled allocation accompanies fewer but more expensive
collections and a larger heap footprint. The small retained heap differences
within each 760 call batch do not establish a leak, absence of all leaks, or

within call maxima. The heap numbers include the local Node/Vite process.

### Arena allocation diagnosis and correction plan

**Status:** Ready for the bounded three file storage correction after the
independent plan gate. The diagnosis changed no repository source or
acceptance harness and ran no acceptance trial.

The same independent Rubber Duck context, with its model unchanged, found no
blocking plan changes. It confirmed the logical/physical identity separation,
transfer and retirement rules, actual storage capture, complete equivalence
and error gates. This is readiness only, not another consensus vote, source
clearance for changed code or cost qualification.

Ten isolated fresh process array probes confirmed an allocation boundary on
the pinned runtime: 16,382 reference slots remained regular allocation;
16,383 used a 131,080 byte large object backing store. The maximum 16,982
slot arena used 135,872 bytes. Its backing store moved to old large object
space after a requested minor collection, remained allocated while the
cleared array was live, and became reclaimable after release. Controls with
29 and 13,264 slots did not use a large object backing store.

The captured workload's arenas range from 16,925 through 16,965 slots, so
every five case arena crosses the observed boundary despite at most 29
simultaneously owned states. This establishes an avoidable allocation
mechanism, not its share of the total slowdown or a universal engine limit.
No heap size or optimization setting was changed to pass a gate.

Two separate CPU profiles, one per variant, are explicitly diagnostic.
Their measured batch sample window attributes approximately 11.6% of B's
sampled time to `evaluateProgram` itself, with additional finalization and
potential work. Inclusive durations overlap, and main thread samples do not
attribute background collector CPU. B also performs about 0.81% more actual
integrations and 25% more forecasts than the four case A baseline; sharing
saved work against an unshared five case matrix, not against four cases.

**Selected hypothesis:** Compile physical arena slots from the existing
final reader lifetimes to remove oversized per observation storage while
retaining the exact prefix computation. Do not also tune retirement loops,
dispatch, scoring or propagation in this experiment.

**Goal:** Size each fresh runtime arena to its actual simultaneous ownership
need, without changing logical prefix identity, forecasts or selector behavior.

**Architecture:** Keep logical nodes and ordered operations intact. Add
immutable physical slot operands during compilation. A transfer inherits its
parent's slot; a fork uses a previously retired slot or allocates the next
slot. Each evaluation still owns a fresh arena and clears it on every exit.

**Stack:** Existing TypeScript, Vitest and Node measurements. No new tooling,
dependency, runtime hook or application change.

> **For agentic workers:** Use `subagent-driven-development` or
> `executing-plans` only after the independent readiness gate.

#### A. Pin storage behavior before implementation

**Modify only:** `tests\fixtures\sunlitPulseTopology.ts`,
`tests\fixtures\sunlitPulsePolicy.ts` and
`tests\unit\sunlitPulseEvaluator.test.ts`.

The frozen scalar reference, portable input fixture and policy warmup counts
remain unchanged. So do all timing cases, integration counts, requested
pulses, the correction allowance, horizon, driver, assets and original
runtime profiles.

- [x] Add and observe failing exact capacity assertions:

```ts
const expectedFourSlots = [14, 18, 22, 16, 18, 17, 16, 15];
const expectedFiveSlots = [17, 20, 29, 20, 23, 22, 20, 19];
```

Zero timing and the existing tiny duplicate path schedule each need three
slots. These are bounds for these declared schedules, not a new global
29 slot limit for every otherwise valid timing input.

- [x] Capture actual numeric length arena construction using a transparent
      test only proxy around the native Array constructor, installed with
      existing Vitest global stubbing only during evaluation and restored
      in `finally`. Compile before installing it, and run reference/assertion
      work after restoring it. Confirm one fresh arena, its actual capacity
      and cleared entries after success or error. An actual large allocation
      must fail, even if a reported counter is merely renamed or reduced.
- [x] Test every compiled read against a logical owner model: distinct live
      owners cannot share a physical slot, only a final reader transfer
      inherits a live parent slot, retired slots may be reused, zero is a
      valid free slot, and every operand is in range. Cover every declared
      held mask and the duplicate path fixture.

#### B. Compile slots without changing prefix identity

- [x] Preserve logical node/parent IDs, depth, symbol, finish row order and
      retirement IDs. Prefix keys remain `logicalParentId * 108 + symbol`.
      Enforce the 18,000 unique integration node limit with logical nodes,
      never with reused slot numbers.
- [x] Assign root slot zero. During compilation, maintain a logical owner
      mapping and deterministic LIFO free list:

```ts
const nodeSlot = operation.takeParent
  ? parentSlot
  : (freeSlots.pop() ?? nextSlot++);
```

Use nullish handling so retired slot zero is not accidentally discarded.
Transfers replace the parent's logical ownership without freeing its
physical slot. In particular, a consumed transfer parent must not appear in
physical retirements or the free list after its slot belongs to the child.
The existing logical retirement projection already excludes consumed
parents; preserve that projection while adding physical operands.

- [x] Add explicitly named physical operands:

```ts
interface AdvanceSlotOperands {
  parentSlot: number;
  nodeSlot: number;
  retireSlots: readonly number[];
}
interface FinishSlotOperands {
  leafSlot: number;
  boundarySlot: number;
  evaluationSlot: number;
  retireSlots: readonly number[];
}
```

Finish releases slots only after all leaf/boundary/evaluation reads.
An already empty immutable retirement array can serve both representations
without creating a second empty array. Discard construction mappings and
free lists before returning the compiled evaluator.

- [x] Require the compiled slot capacity to equal its symbolic peak owned
      states. Preserve all existing timing validation and node 18,001 rejection.
      This correction must not change the logical operation sequence.

#### C. Change runtime addressing, not computation

- [x] Allocate one new arena at the compiled compact capacity for every
      evaluation. Do not introduce a runtime node map, free list, pool,
      observation cache or reused arena.
- [x] Use the predecoded physical operands. Read the parent before clearing
      its slot; a transfer still removes the parent entry before advancement,
      even when parent and child use the same physical slot. Preserve the
      five array fork copies, immediate finalization, detached outputs and
      unconditional `finally` cleanup.
- [x] Keep the public evaluator/matrix/work type shapes unchanged.
      `stateIndexSlots` continues to mean actual arena length, so its numerical
      bounds deliberately shrink. Logical prefix count remains
      `integrations + 1`, reported separately in evidence.
      Integrations, expanded ticks, state allocations, peak owned states and
      final live states must not change.

#### D. Correctness, errors and unchanged cost gate

- [x] Retain identical error objects and ordering at motion calls 125, 126
      and 179. Add failure and success after a slot has actually been
      retired and reused. Confirm constructor captured arenas are cleared,
      no partial matrix escapes, earlier results stay unchanged and a
      different subsequent observation matches the reference.
- [x] Retain all 34,200 complete matrix/reference comparisons and fish
      aliases, every held mask/root, recovery `[10, null, 10]`, direct motion
      spy counts, zero timing, horizon, malformed/sparse input order and
      actual work budget assertions.
- [x] Preserve all 152 decisions, 15,200 public forecasts, retained binary
      oracle and five complete runtime profile comparisons. Run all current
      486 cases plus new cases, typecheck and scoped lint/format. Update only
      intentionally changed storage expectations.
- [x] Obtain an independent source gate, then let the parent run one new
      source experiment using the unchanged hardened worker and fixed
      A/B, B/A, A/B protocol. Keep every trial, the same warmup and 760 call
      batches, and all three unrounded pooled cost criteria. No forced GC,
      profiling or engine tuning belongs in timing acceptance.
- [x] Keep failed v1 immutable. Use a new experiment identity and new source
      fingerprints, not a replacement or unchanged rerun. Separately inspect
      collector behavior and extra immutable slot metadata residency.
      Diagnostics cannot override the cost criteria.

**Keep or discard rule:** Any exactness, storage, actual work or cost failure
keeps publication blocked. Revisit this hypothesis explicitly rather than
adding unrelated optimizations or widening the gate.

**Evidence:** `evaluator-diagnosis-v1-recommendation.json`,
`evaluator-diagnosis-v1-arena-space.json`,
`evaluator-diagnosis-v1-cpu-analysis.json` and their retained probes/profiles.
All six source fingerprints and the hardened worker digest remained unchanged
during diagnosis. Task 14 and all later gates remain open.

### Course timing C selection implementation plan

**Status:** Ready for the bounded six file implementation after the
independent plan gate. No selector variant was executed during design.
The new oracle provenance contract below must also hold before cost
authorization.

The same independent Rubber Duck context, with its model unchanged, accepted
the scope, independent reference, actual work accounting and behavioral stop
conditions. It requested explicit content and producer fingerprints for the
new expected decision oracle; that requirement is included below. This is
readiness, not source, liveness, cost or native clearance.

**Goal:** Append only the recorded Course timing C to P/L/Z/E, then establish
changed behavior and complete selector cost evidence.

**Hypothesis:** Including C in the unchanged selection reducer will reject
the demonstrated unsafe command when an existing safe candidate is available.
This may improve traversal under repeated C timing; the fixed physical
regression must establish that outcome rather than assuming it.

**Architecture:** Reuse the compact evaluator and existing selection reducer.
Keep the public three case `sunlitPulseScenarios()` API unchanged because
it also defines the correction allowance. Add an independent selection
reference outside the frozen scalar source, and distinguish logical work
expansion from actual integration calls in the returned decision.

**Stack:** Existing TypeScript, Vitest, original runtime/GLBs and the
established Node measurement approach. No dependency or application change.

> **For agentic workers:** Use `subagent-driven-development` or
> `executing-plans` only after the independent readiness gate.

#### B. Independent selection reference and retained controls
