## Task 15: public documentation and Pages release

**Goal:** publish the normal tested game at
`https://ridermw.github.io/reef-rush/`, document its actual behavior and original
assets, and verify the exact deployed artifact through native browser behavior.
This remains a plan until Task 14 is complete and this readiness gate passes.

**Observed preflight:** the repository is public. Pages is already configured
in workflow mode with HTTPS enforced. The `github-pages` environment permits
only branch `main` and has no other reported protection rule type. Preserve
those safeguards, rather than recreating the site or weakening protection.
The canonical URL currently returns HTTP 404, and the environment deployment
query returned no entries. Repository homepage is not set.

**Choice:** extend the accepted CI workflow with a narrowly privileged deploy
job and a separate read only live verification job. Do not grant Pages/OIDC
credentials to dependency installation or application test jobs. Reuse the
normal artifact already produced after the complete gates; do not rebuild a
different bundle in the deployment job. No separate release framework,
preview deployment, custom domain, asset regeneration, gameplay change, cloud
save service, or human quality score is part of this task.

### Public documentation

- Rewrite README around the actual original solo underwater time trial and
  live link, three available courses, earned medal unlock chain, checkpoints,
  pearls, local records, settings, recovery and diagnostics. Remove obsolete
  planned architecture/progress text and unsupported claims of polished feel.
- Correct controls from implemented input: W speeds up, S slows toward zero
  rather than reversing, A/D or horizontal arrows steer, vertical arrows pitch,
  optional mouse movement steers, Space dashes, Shift brakes, and Escape
  pauses/resumes outside dialogs. Dialog Escape closes without resuming.
  Remove the nonexistent R shortcut and boost pickup claim.
- Use portable pinned Node plus `npm ci` guidance, not the obsolete macOS NVM
  snippet or unlocked `npm install`. Explain dev/build/preview, focused tests,
  full validation and explicit Playwright browser installation. Blender is
  required only for explicit authoring, not ordinary setup/build/tests.
- Explain local browser storage and its limits: no account/cloud leaderboard,
  settings version two with protected version one normalization, separate
  progress storage, explicit invalid-save backup/replacement, no valid reset or
  backup import, and protected unsupported saves. Do not promise hard process
  exit or blocked storage durability. State tested Chromium/headless scope,
  desktop keyboard requirements, WebGL 2, graphics recovery, reduced effects,
  and unobserved human visual/gameplay judgments.
- Keep code MIT and original assets CC BY 4.0 with existing attribution.
  Preserve the detailed source/profile facts in `ASSET-LICENSE.md`; replace
  obsolete instructions to copy only the new Blacksmoker pair with a current
  separate-directory reproduction/comparison procedure for all eight files.
  No `.blend` archive, downloaded artwork or machine installation path is
  required. Do not change artwork or reviewed hash baselines.

### Artifact provenance

Add a small Node core utility, `tools\write-build-info.mjs`, that takes the
explicit source commit and the normal output directory. Before writing, require
a forty character lowercase hexadecimal commit and a readable `index.html`.
Write deterministic `build-info.json` with exactly `version: 1`, `commit`,
and `indexSha256`, hashing the actual HTML bytes with SHA-256. Do not include
environment dumps, credentials, local paths, user data or variable timestamps.
Fail visibly on bad input/read/write; never invent a revision or success result.
This is artifact identification, not a supply chain signature or attestation.

The checked normal bundle receives this metadata before Pages upload. The live
gate must match the expected workflow source commit and the hash of the
canonical HTTP HTML bytes, not serialized/mutated DOM content or a cache-busted
alternate URL. It also fetches all eight original GLBs and compares bytes to
the checked out source. This prevents a successful response or old functional
site from being mistaken for the newly published artifact.

### Deployment and verification

Verified official pins:

| Action                          | Release  | Verified immutable commit                  |
| ------------------------------- | -------- | ------------------------------------------ |
| `actions/upload-pages-artifact` | `v5.0.0` | `fc324d3547104276b827a68afc52ff2a11cc49c9` |
| `actions/deploy-pages`          | `v5.0.1` | `368f82528645a54fb793d4d04e342629a3f51346` |

The upload action explicitly supports Windows packaging and internally pins
Node24 `upload-artifact` v7.0.0. The deploy action declares Node24 and requires
the previously uploaded artifact plus `pages: write` and `id-token: write`.
The site is already in workflow mode; a configuration action is not required
by the inspected deployment prerequisites.

- Upload the `github-pages` artifact only after normal build checks/provenance,
  and only for this repository's main push or main manual dispatch. Pull
  requests and feature dispatches remain validation only.
- A dependent deploy job gets only the required Pages/OIDC permissions and
  targets the existing `github-pages` environment. Do not run npm or app code
  in that privileged job. Preserve the main branch environment policy.
- A dependent live verification job has only `contents: read`, checks out the
  same workflow SHA, selects pinned Node, restores locked public dependencies,
  and installs the pinned Chromium. It receives
  `REEF_RUSH_EXPECTED_COMMIT` from that workflow SHA and cannot deploy.
- Serialize the entire main pipeline through deployment and verification,
  not merely the deploy step, so a newer run cannot replace the site while
  the prior run verifies it. Use a stable ref based concurrency group; do not
  cancel a running main publication. Pull request cancellation can remain
  independent. Checkpoint-only main pushes must not recursively redeploy
  merely because the handoff records the prior passed release. Exclude only
  `docs/handoffs/2026-09-04-reef-rush.md` from automatic push triggering if this
  exception has not already landed in Task 14; all source changes, pull
  requests and manual gates remain covered.
- Add `playwright.live.config.ts`, with no local web servers and the canonical
  HTTPS base. A live metadata project runs
  `tests\browser\live-deployment.spec.ts`; dependent live production coverage
  reuses the normal `production.spec.ts` scenarios. Explicitly exclude the
  live-only file from ordinary local acceptance. Do not accidentally inherit
  localhost project URLs or the acceptance project.
- Require the expected commit before live requests. Use a bounded five minute
  readiness poll on the canonical metadata/HTML for normal CDN propagation,
  then verify all original assets and run native production controls/drawing/
  settings/diagnostics/teardown with no acceptance global. These isolated
  browser contexts never touch the user's actual browser saves. Keep TLS
  validation; do not hide failures with retries or security bypass flags.
  Limit each HTTP request to ten seconds or the smaller remaining readiness
  budget. The deadline includes requests and waits; it does not restart per
  attempt. Give the metadata test a bounded seven minute budget for readiness
  plus the eight asset reads. Malformed metadata and permanent errors fail
  visibly; only propagation states such as 404, stale revision, or temporarily
  mismatched HTML are eligible for the readiness poll.
- Bind every live production page and reload to the expected artifact, not
  just the preceding API probe. A shared automatic production test fixture
  observes actual main-document response bytes and validates their digest
  against metadata carrying the expected commit. It must observe at least one
  canonical document per test, await all validation work before accepting the
  result, and surface asynchronous failures. Do not intercept or replace
  document content. A stale native navigation must fail even if its gameplay
  still works. Preserve ordinary local production behavior; reject a live
  expected commit paired with a localhost/noncanonical base, or a canonical
  live base without the expected commit, before requests.
- Retain live JSON/HTML/traces under a separate evidence artifact name.
  Record exact source, workflow/deployment result, canonical URL and observed
  live outcomes. After success, set the previously empty repository homepage
  to the verified URL. A green deploy action alone is not completed release
  evidence.

```text
read-only checks -> normal bundle + commit/index digest -> Pages artifact
  -> trusted main deploy (Pages/OIDC only)
  -> read-only canonical metadata/assets gate
  -> dependent native production browser scenarios
  -> public source checkpoint + live URL, with observed limits
```

### Implementation and acceptance steps

**Files:** `README.md`, `ASSET-LICENSE.md`,
`.github\workflows\windows-baseline.yml`, `tools\write-build-info.mjs`,
`tests\bootstrap\buildInfo.test.mjs`, the bootstrap script in `package.json`,
`playwright.live.config.ts`, the local exclusion in `playwright.config.ts`,
`tests\unit\playwrightLiveConfig.test.ts`, relevant CI/config contract tests,
`tests\browser\live-deployment.spec.ts`,
`tests\fixtures\livePublication.ts`,
`tests\unit\livePublication.test.ts`, and a shared automatic fixture in
`tests\browser\productionTest.ts` imported by the production scenarios.

- [ ] Observe failing metadata utility cases before its code: exact byte hash,
      deterministic fields, invalid/missing revision, missing HTML, read/write
      failure and no success-shaped fallback. Keep bootstrap dependency free.
- [ ] Observe failing configuration/workflow contract cases for live project
      isolation, HTTPS URL, expected source, metadata dependency, absence of
      local servers, least privilege/main-only promotion, retained safeguards
      and main publication serialization. Then implement those paths without
      weakening the existing complete validation/browser gate.
- [ ] Observe deterministic failing provenance gate regressions before the
      helper/fixture implementation: missing expected SHA before any request,
      stale commit, malformed metadata, correct commit with wrong HTML digest,
      asset mismatch, deadline exhaustion, transient responses becoming valid,
      and stale actual navigation after a successful API probe. Include
      missing document evidence, reload/additional-page coverage, asynchronous
      rejection propagation, URL/mode mismatch, per-request limits and the
      single overall deadline. Use fake requests/clocks within existing test
      frameworks, not a new service or altered live response.
- [ ] Update public documentation from verified implementation, preserving
      attribution and distinguishing measured behavior from human quality.
      Documentation does not need unrelated runtime tests.
- [ ] Run focused utility/config contracts, types and scoped lint/format.
      Verify normal metadata output locally and its unchanged original assets.
      Do not claim live acceptance against the currently unpublished site.
- [ ] Obtain an independent implementation publication gate, push reviewed
      source and verify feature CI without deployment. Integrate main and
      inspect the actual trusted artifact, deployment and live verification
      jobs on the exact published source.
- [ ] Independently confirm canonical HTTP/source/assets and native production
      behavior locally when network access permits. If local network policy
      prevents it, use actual hosted live browser evidence and label the local
      limitation, never bypass TLS or claim an unobserved local result.
- [ ] Publish the final handoff with source/result/limitations, verify remote
      persistence, and confirm no user-owned data or files were discarded.
      Only then is the autonomous objective complete.

| Field      | Initial release experiment                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Baseline   | Public repository and main-only workflow Pages setup exist, but the canonical URL returns HTTP 404 and public README still describes planned systems and incorrect controls.                                                         |
| Hypothesis | Publishing only the checked normal artifact with explicit revision/digest evidence and independent live browser coverage can establish a truthful, reproducible public release.                                                      |
| Metric     | Protected main-only deployment, expected commit and canonical HTML digest, all eight original GLBs matching source, native production scenarios without test hooks, correct public docs/provenance and persistent remote checkpoint. |
| Result     | Preflight and plan only. No deployment or live gameplay result is claimed.                                                                                                                                                           |
| Decision   | Readiness accepted after native document binding and negative provenance coverage repairs. Preserve original assets and do not inflate human graphics or gameplay judgments.                                                         |

The initial release plan review found two medium proof gaps: a separate native
navigation could receive different CDN HTML than the readiness probe, and the
new provenance gate lacked an explicit negative behavior matrix. The plan now
requires actual main-document response binding and deterministic failure/
deadline regressions. The same reviewer cleared both repairs in a bounded
informed followup, not another independent review or consensus. CI and release
each used one fresh generic reviewer context with high effort requested, no
model override, and no confirmed provider diversity. These are readiness
decisions only; no new CI execution or live release result is claimed.

## Completed Task 12: Blacksmoker Run

**Status:** all four slices are reviewed and integrated into `main`. Source
`10aa891` passes the hosted Windows gate. The merged state passes 74 focused
tests across nine files; the exact source previously passed full local
validation and all 28 browser scenarios.
The existing autonomous authorization covers execution; no interactive design
approval or linked worktree isolation is implied.

**Goal:** finish the third original expedition and its earned progression
without changing the established simulation, persistence, or ownership model.

**Architecture:** reuse version 1 course definitions, explicit lazy loading,
SceneRuntime, original glTF assets, and the existing progression chain.
Generated calibration precedes original assets, which precede activation.
Use the existing TypeScript, Three.js, Rapier, Vitest, Playwright, and Blender
toolchain without new dependencies.

**Choice:** a deeper volcanic slalom with rising currents and three offset
gates adds a distinct final course. A color reskin would not add enough route
variety; new lava, damage, or smoke simulation would expand the physics and
ownership surface unnecessarily. Static original chimneys and mineral forms
provide the theme, with no new gameplay mechanic.

One hypothesis was tested: native calls can span multiple physics steps, so
fixed error thresholds overshoot yaw alignment and absolute pitch targets.
The baseline contained intervals up to 43 steps. The new policy evaluates the
nine steering combinations on copies through the real motion function over
the observed interval, bounded to one through sixty steps. It applies only
the chosen native keys to the real game. Speed, throttle, braking, currents,
dash usage, and normalized controls were not retuned.

change. Twelve policy tests cover the long interval correction, contrasting
single step behavior, angle wrapping, interval bounds, exclusive keys, and
input immutability. Parent validation passes those and the five unchanged

## Task 11: Kelpworks implementation plan

**Status:** All three slices are complete, reviewed, and integrated into main.
Continue with Task 12.

**Goal:** make the second course a complete, original, finishable expedition
with measured medal calibration, protected progression, and the existing
runtime and audio ownership guarantees.

**Architecture:** author one additional course through the existing schema,
loader, SceneRuntime, and glTF profile. First prove its route with generated
presentation, then replace that presentation with original Blender output,
then expose it through the existing earned unlock. No new physics rules,
dependencies, timers, renderer, progression schema, or save key.

**Choice:** a longer kelp slalom with crossing currents and a depth change
adds variety without inventing new obstacle mechanics. A Sunlit reskin would
not add a distinct route; a new kelp physics system would expand scope and
invalidate established collision guarantees. Use static original fronds,
existing currents, and two rotating gates.

## Task 10: shell, settings, audio, and feedback

**Status:** Settings, controls, owned audio, and shell integration are complete
in `87c8be2` and integrated into `main`. Local, independent, and hosted run
`33950680326` gates pass. Decisions are autonomous and all
execution stays headless. Preserve strict TDD, one independent Rubber Duck gate
per ordinary slice, and the existing handoff rather than new planning files.

**Goal:** finish the usable single course experience with clear controls,
protected preferences, original audio, actionable results, and restrained
feedback. Do not implement later courses, quality presets, save recovery,
deployment, or broad compatibility work here.

**Architecture:** a separate immutable settings store serves the shell and
host through subscriptions, never through frame updates. A lazy owned Web
Audio engine accepts discrete cues and gameplay state. GameHost translates
actual scene events into audio and bounded HUD feedback; physics and race
rules remain independent. Settings live in `reef-rush.settings`, never in
the progress record.

**Alternatives:** a third party audio package or downloaded soundtrack adds
dependencies and provenance without solving the ownership problem. Audio tied
to React renders can duplicate cues. Choose original synthesized cues and a
small event driven service. Keep existing scene/race ownership rather than
adding a second application loop.

### Slice 10A: protected preferences and control orientation (complete)

- Add `src\settings\settings.ts`, `settingsStorage.ts`, and `SettingsStore.ts`
  with focused `tests\unit\settings.test.ts` and storage/store tests.
- Version 1 defaults: master volume 0.4, sound effects enabled, ambience
  disabled, mouse steering enabled, sensitivity 1, pitch inversion disabled,
  reduced effects disabled. Volume is in `[0, 1]`, sensitivity in `[0.25, 2]`;
  flags must be actual booleans. Validate complete settings and patches before
  changing state. Preserve malformed, future version, and unavailable storage
  with an explicit session preference notice.
- Field names are `masterVolume`, `sfxEnabled`, `musicEnabled`,
  `mouseSteering`, `mouseSensitivity`, `invertMouseY`, and `reducedMotion`.
  Pitch inversion affects the mouse only; arrow up remains climb.
- Changes apply immediately in memory. Persistence is best effort with an
  explicit failure state and whole configuration last writer semantics.
  Do not claim cross tab merging or modify progress storage. No startup write.
- Add camera relative keyboard and pointer regressions before changing input.
  Default right input must turn toward camera right; default up input must
  raise the nose. Repair any demonstrated mapping inversion at the input
  boundary, preserving the mathematical motion model and course data.
- Extend `InputController` with validated preferences and clear stale pointer
  deltas on changes. Mouse disable and sensitivity affect only pointer input;
  invert pitch is explicit. Preserve throttle, braking, dash, Escape, native
  editable controls, normalized axes, and lifecycle cleanup.
- If input mapping changes, update the real keyboard traversal driver to use
  the corrected mapping. Keep the same path/finish assertions, not a bypass.

### Slice 10B: original Web Audio service (complete)

- Add `src\game\audio\AudioEngine.ts` and focused unit tests. Own at most one
  live context per host; healthy replays reuse it. Create it only from an
  explicit user gesture and only when audio is enabled. Do not rely on
  autoplay or import audio through Three.js.
- Use original quiet oscillator/envelope cues for dash, checkpoint, pearl,
  finish, collision/hazard, breach, and splashdown. Ambience is optional,
  initially off. Cap concurrent voices and throttle repetitive contact cues;
  no audio frame loop or unbounded timer collection.
- Apply master volume smoothly. Muting, pause, hidden focus loss, and title
  navigation silence voices immediately; ambience runs only while playing.
  Resume never replays stale cues. Unlock/resume completions are fenced against
  later mute, pause, navigation, or disposal.
- Audio is optional: unavailable/blocked/failed audio has a visible status but
  never changes race results or stops gameplay. Retain errors and cleanup
  ownership rather than swallowing failed node release or context close.
  Define asynchronous disposal so the host never treats a promise as a
  synchronous release callback.
- Keep audio shutdown promises/errors separate from fatal runtime cleanup.
  Start silence/closure before awaiting pending saves. Audio cleanup can be
  retried and observed without blocking course loading; final host disposal
  reports an unresolved audio close. Test this while a progress lock is held.
- Test gesture gating, real API shape through injected deterministic contexts,
  volume bounds, voice caps, cue throttling, silence, stale async completion,
  cleanup retry, and idempotence. Browser evidence later observes native
  context/voice lifecycle, not a claim that a human heard or liked the sound.

### Slice 10C: shell integration and useful feedback (complete)

- Main owns one settings store and one host. Add a native accessible settings
  dialog from title and pause; closing settings must not resume a paused run.
  Focus stays in the modal and returns to its opener. Escape and native range/
  checkbox keys must not leak into gameplay.
- Explicitly give Settings ownership of keyboard events; native modality alone
  does not stop the host window Escape handler. Cover focus on the dialog,
  close button, range, and checkbox.
- Replace implementation jargon and stale generated artwork copy. Keep an
  ocean expedition identity with cream text, reef teal, warm medal accents,
  and available local font fallbacks; no font or media service requests.
- Share time formatting between HUD/results. Show earned medal, pearls, best
  time context, genuine new records/unlocks, Race again, and course selection.
  Restart and course navigation retain protected pending progress saves.
- Capture immutable achievement metadata before updating progress. Any record
  badge is explicitly relative to progress known at finish, not a claim about
  unseen cross tab records. Test ties, slower replays, and a queued save that
  discovers a better stored record.
- Feed sound and restrained textual cues from actual fish/race events. Rate
  limit HUD publication to the existing cadence; announce meaningful race
  events without flooding assistive technology with continuous collisions.
  Keep feedback outside the central fish/route view and intercept pointer
  input on any opaque overlay. Avoid flashing effects.
- Consume events once per fixed step, including terminal events, before the
  transition. Keep a bounded prioritized HUD cue: finish, checkpoint, pearl,
  hazard/contact, then movement. Clear it on replay. Cover multiple events in
  one catchup frame. A short finish cue may complete on results; ambience may
  not continue there.
- Wire settings through host/input/audio subscriptions. Reduced effects, also
  honoring the system preference, suppress decorative motion and CSS
  transitions without changing simulation or essential camera tracking.
- Extend only immutable test diagnostics for effective preferences, audio
  status/counts and last cue. Normal production must expose no hook or mutable
  gameplay operations.

### Slice 10D: acceptance and publication

- Add browser regressions before integration for settings persistence and
  paused modal keyboard behavior, replay/results actions, corrected input
  direction if needed, and native audio unlock/silence.
- Retain all 14 Task 9 browser scenarios and exact headless traversal. Observe
  real keyboard course completion under any corrected input mapping.
- Validate three lifecycle cycles, settings/progress isolation, no duplicate
  audio contexts, no active voices on title, and no runtime owners after host
  disposal. Run the complete local milestone gate and hosted Windows baseline.
- Record baseline, hypothesis, metrics, results, and keep/discard decisions
  here. Human gameplay feel, art quality, and perceived sound quality remain
  unobserved. Publish before starting Task 11.

Plan review: one independent generic Rubber Duck context cleared scope and
identified the four integration clarifications now recorded above. High effort
was requested. No model diversity or consensus is claimed. The implementation
below covers the shutdown, modal, provenance, and event clarifications.

### Slice 10A checkpoint

`src\settings\settings.ts` exports strict readonly settings/defaults and
patch/input preference schemas. `settingsStorage.ts` exports `readSettings`,
`saveSettings`, `SettingsRead`, and typed `SettingsStorageError` with
`unavailable`, `invalid-save`, or `write-failed` codes. Only
`reef-rush.settings` is touched.

`createSettingsStore(provider?)` exposes `getState()`, `subscribe()`, and
`update(patch)`. Stable immutable snapshots contain `settings`, `status`,
`notice`, and `error`. Status is `empty`, `loaded`, `invalid`, `unavailable`,
`saved`, or `session-only`. No startup write occurs. Validation precedes state
changes; valid changes survive persistence failure in the current session.
An empty patch retries persistence. Invalid raw settings observed at write
are preserved, and a later update can recover when storage becomes valid.
There is no cross tab coordination or merge guarantee.

`InputController` accepts optional constructor `preferences` and a public
`setPreferences(input)` that validates the complete mouse preference object.
Changes clear pending pointer deltas but retain held keys and queued actions.
Disable/sensitivity/inversion affect only the mouse.

The parent observed ten failing real InputController/motion/camera cases:
keyboard and mouse directions were inverted in the chase camera frame.
The repaired boundary preserves the mathematical model: `steerX > 0`
increases yaw and turns camera left for +Z forward; `steerY > 0` raises the
nose. D/right input now yields negative yaw, and up input positive pitch.
Arrow up always climbs, including when mouse pitch inversion is enabled.
Physics, course data, tuning, medals, and direct headless driver inputs did
not change.

The first browser pair exposed a separate driver flaw: one run finished with
three pearls because the driver advanced on depth alone. An unchanged passing
rerun was not accepted as a fix. The route policy now requires observed
checkpoint/pearl milestones as well as depth, retains missed pearl targets,
and approaches missed checkpoint planes from six units upstream. Nine then
seven focused failures preceded the policy fixes.

Parent browser recovery deliberately missed the first pearl using native keys
at `[-5.108269784569209, -4, 20.733232291568914]`. The driver recovered that
pearl and a later missed pearl, then finished with four checkpoints and four
pearls: **1,787 steps, 29,776.348096178986 ms, bronze**. No teleport, forced
finish, clock mutation, or mutable hook was used. The two existing storage
races also passed after the policy repair. The recovery test uses the existing
150 second scenario budget; its internal driver deadline remains 120 seconds.

One independent generic Rubber Duck review cleared spec and quality with no
Critical or Important findings. Its Minor timeout consistency note was applied.
High effort was requested; no model diversity or consensus is claimed.

Parent final evidence: **183 focused tests**, full lint/format checks, types
and production build pass. Browser inventory is now 15 scenarios; this slice
ran the two storage races and the new actual recovery case, not the full
15 case milestone gate. Normal production `dist` was restored after browsing.

### Slice 10B checkpoint

`c3da6a9` adds `src\game\audio\AudioEngine.ts`, `AudioGraph.ts`, and
`tests\unit\AudioEngine.test.ts`. Synthesis is original sine/triangle pitch
and envelope data in `AudioGraph.ts`; no package, soundtrack, network request,
or audio asset was added.

`createAudioEngine(deps?)` exposes `unlock()`, `setSettings(input)`,
`setPhase(phase)`, `play(cue)`, `getState()`/`getSnapshot()`, `subscribe()`,
`dispose()`, and `retryCleanup()`. Settings use the existing complete schema.
Phases are `idle`, `playing`, `paused`, and `results`. Cues are `dash`,
`checkpoint`, `pearl`, `finish`, `collision`, `hazard`, `breach`, and
`splashdown`. Call `unlock()` directly inside a trusted UI gesture before
awaiting scene loading. The default requires observable native transient
activation; tests inject native shaped context ports and a gesture predicate.

Construction, settings, phases, and cue requests never allocate a context.
Healthy replays retain one context. There are at most eight effect voices and
one optional ambience voice; the latter renews bounded four second envelopes
through native `onended`, not a timer or frame loop. Contact cooldown is
250 ms. Finish may replace the oldest lower priority effect at capacity.
Volume ramps take 30 ms; mute, pause, and idle stop voices immediately.
Results preserve only finish effects, never ambience. Cues are never queued.
Stale resume completions cannot revive cancelled intent.

Snapshots are stable and immutable. Status is `locked`, `unlocking`, `ready`,
`blocked`, `unavailable`, `failed`, `disposing`, or `disposed`; `ready` means
backend readiness, not audible playback. Diagnostics expose context ownership,
owned nodes, active effect/ambience counts, emitted/drop/replacement counts,
last cue/drop reason, and pending unlock/cleanup. `pendingUnlock` may remain
true after successful disposal because shutdown must not await a blocked
resume. Closed context/node ownership is still zero.

Failed synchronous node releases remain owned; asynchronous context closure
is managed separately. `dispose()` starts silence/closure immediately and
rejects `AggregateError` if release fails. Repeated disposal returns the same
attempt; only `retryCleanup()` retries failed work. Live recovery returns to
`locked`, requiring another trusted unlock. The latest listener exception is
retained in bounded `observerErrors` with an explicit notice, separately from
backend `cause`, cleanup errors, and lifecycle outcomes.

The initial independent Rubber Duck reviewer found two Medium defects:
blocked resume could strand a failed silence operation, and listener failures
could overwrite successful disposal or the actual close error. The parent
observed one failing cleanup regression and four failing observer regressions
before production fixes. A bounded followup cleared both. Two generic reviewer
contexts were used because synchronous reviewers cannot receive followup
messages; high effort was requested, with no model diversity or consensus
claimed.

Parent final evidence: **134 focused tests** (75 audio and 59 settings),
typecheck, scoped ESLint, and scoped Prettier pass. The followup independently
ran the 10 relevant recovery tests. Native browser integration is covered
below; human audibility and perceived quality remain unobserved.

### Slice 10C and local acceptance checkpoint

`87c8be2` wires one Main owned settings store and host outside StrictMode.
The native Settings dialog owns keyboard events explicitly, contains focus,
and restores its opener without resuming a paused race. Storage failure
notices remain visible, and settings never write the progress key.
`GameHost.setSettingsOpen()`, `unlockAudio()`, `getAudioNotice()`,
`subscribeAudio()`, and `retryAudioCleanup()` provide the shell boundary.
Audio notice subscriptions return a primitive notice, not frame/cue counters.

The title, HUD, pause, loading, error, and results copy now use the expedition
presentation. Fonts are local fallbacks and title artwork is original inline
SVG; no remote media or heavy gameplay assets load on title. HUD feedback is
outside the central fish view. Results retain real race values and provide
Race again and Choose another course. Pending protected saves survive replay
and navigation.

`finishAchievements()` captures immutable records and unlock eligibility
before progress changes. First completion is distinct from a strictly faster
record; ties and slower runs do not earn the time badge. Record claims state
that they use records available at finish. A later queued save discovering
better stored history does not rewrite this provenance.

`createRunFeedback()` consumes actual fish/race events once per fixed step,
including terminal events. It keeps one cue for 1.8 seconds with priority
finish, checkpoint, pearl, contact/hazard, then movement. Audio receives the
discrete events independently; meaningful race announcements are polite.
The existing 10 Hz HUD publication bound remains. Replays clear stale cues.

Effective preferences combine the explicit reduced effects flag with native
system reduced motion. Both visual paths expose `setReducedMotion()` through
SceneRuntime. Decorative particles and swim animation stop; simulation,
camera tracking, markers, gate transforms, and owned resource counts do not.
Host diagnostics add immutable preferences, audio state, and feedback while
preserving the existing runtime resource shape and sole `getSnapshot()` hook.
Normal production remains hook free.

Audio shutdown begins before awaiting a pending progress lock or resume.
Its async owner and failures remain separate from fatal runtime cleanup.
Optional audio failure does not block course loading. A failed close is
observable and explicitly retryable; final host disposal reports unresolved
work. One independent generic Rubber Duck context found a stale live retry
error that incorrectly rejected a later successful terminal disposal. The
parent observed the failing regression before clearing that error only on
successful audio shutdown. The same reviewer cleared the bounded followup
and independently ran three recovery regressions. High effort was requested;
no model diversity or consensus is claimed.

The parent wrote and observed four browser failures before integration:
missing settings from title/pause, no native audio context, and missing replay
after a real finish. New native settings, modal, audio, and replay cases now
pass alongside all 15 existing cases. A bare native audio experiment corrected
the observer, not the outcome: Chromium reports `contextChanged: closed` after
`close()` without necessarily emitting `contextWillBeDestroyed`. The test
observes both and requires every created context to close.

## Task 9: original Sunlit assets

**Status:** assets are complete in `3f78fcf`, the cache in `a0492a3`, and
scene integration in `2f62cfb`. The milestone is integrated into `main`.
Local, hosted, and independent review gates are clear. Task 10 is also complete.
Use Superpowers with strict TDD and one independent Rubber Duck gate per slice.
The user authorized autonomous decisions and headless execution.

**Goal:** replace Sunlit's blockout presentation with original authored assets
without changing its route, collision dimensions, race rules, or medal times.

**Architecture:** Blender 4.5 LTS scripts generate ordinary self contained GLB
files with lit color materials and no external images or decoder requirements.
A leased Three.js asset cache owns shared geometry, materials, and textures.
Scene instances own transforms, animation mixers, effects, and their leases.
Collision assets contain simple named proxies, not detailed coral triangles.

**Alternatives:** keeping generated geometry would preserve behavior but would
not deliver the asset pipeline. Detailed triangle collision would make artwork
control movement and complicate the accepted continuous collision contract.
Choose original visuals with validated primitive proxies; decoration never
silently becomes a gameplay obstacle.

**Art direction:** an original small orange and cream Sunfin with a teal dorsal
accent, expressive eyes, separate fins, and a looping swim animation. Sunlit uses
warm sand terraces, rounded limestone, branching peach coral, lavender fans,
and jade seagrass around the existing open route. A reusable reef kit supplies
props. Rings, pearls, live gate motion, and readable route colors remain
authoritative gameplay feedback. No copied characters or third party artwork.

### Current asset and scene contracts

The exporter uses a fixed seed, explicit meters, glTF +Y up and game forward +Z.
The four self contained files have no external images or decoder dependency.
Budgets are 2 MiB per GLB, 5 MiB combined, 100,000 course triangles, 256 course
mesh nodes, and 10,000 fish triangles. Validation also caps decoded work before
allocating accessor arrays. These are technical caps, not art quality scores.

`CourseDefinition.visuals` is a strict generated/glTF union. The glTF variant
uses `visualAsset` and `collisionAsset`, relative to `public\assets`, with
canonical distinct paths. Sunlit requests exactly its visual GLB, collision
GLB, and `fish/sunfin.glb`, and only after the player starts loading a course.
The prop kit is already instanced into the course file and is not fetched again.

`staticSolidContract.mjs` shares pure primitive metadata/surface validation
between the offline validator and browser code. `validateLoadedCourseAsset.ts`
checks the loaded profile, identities, every static object, dimensions, world
transforms, geometry, winding, and closure. Existing CourseDefinition
primitives still drive Rapier. Neither loader disagreement nor failure silently
falls back to generated art.

`SceneRuntimeDependencies.createVisuals` can return `SceneVisuals` or a promise.
An optional `assetCache` supports isolated owned integrations. The default path
selects the original renderer for glTF definitions and retains generated
fixtures. Heavy imports remain in the lazy scene graph.

`createOriginalSceneVisuals` observes every acquisition with `allSettled` and
owns every successful late lease. Identical `ConstructionCleanupError` objects
from coalesced requests are deduplicated before registration. Mixers stop and
uncache before fish leases release. A failed child is retained for explicit
retry, never implicitly retried by the original catch. Existing GameHost
cancellation waits for loading and owns any late scene or cleanup error.

`createRaceMarkers`, `visualResources`, and `underwaterEnvironment` are shared
by original and generated rendering. Gates follow live Rapier transforms;
checkpoint and pearl feedback follows race state. Decorative mesh camera
exclusions are preserved while static solids remain obstructions. Scene
environment restoration and all resource releases remain retryable.

Sunfin swim and 56 deterministic ambient/current particles use bounded
presentation clocks, including for huge finite frame deltas. Paused/finished
presentation freezes them. Shared asset materials and clips remain immutable;
there is no new React frame state, simulation randomness, or postprocessing
dependency.

| Hypothesis | Authored glTF presentation with validated primitive proxies can improve visual specificity without altering course behavior or resource ownership.                                                                                                |
| Metric     | All asset caps and proxy contracts pass; actual input completes the course; browser lifecycle and production gates remain intact.                                                                                                                 |
| Result     | Parent full validation passes 1,057 Vitest tests and 3 bootstrap tests. All 14 browser cases pass on original assets; exact headless traversal remains 1,317 steps. Two desktop images were viewed. Hosted Windows baseline `33945081394` passed. |
| Decision   | Keep `2f62cfb`. Runtime behavior and ownership gates pass; human graphics quality and gameplay feel remain unobserved.                                                                                                                            |

The requested high effort review added the explicit asynchronous rollback

### Slice 9A checkpoint

`assets\source\build_assets.py` and `sunlit-assets.json` are the editable
originals. `npm run assets:generate -- --blender "<executable>" --output-root
"<directory>"` runs headless Blender and validates the output. The executable
defaults to `blender`; `BLENDER` is also supported. The script's line endings
were normalized to the repository LF policy before staging.

| Asset                                 |  Bytes | Instantiated triangles | Mesh nodes | Primitive draws |
| ------------------------------------- | -----: | ---------------------: | ---------: | --------------: |
| `fish/sunfin.glb`                     | 45,008 |                  1,912 |         10 |              11 |
| `props/reef-kit.glb`                  | 41,264 |                  1,512 |          4 |               4 |
| `courses/sunlit-shoals.visual.glb`    | 78,696 |                 19,824 |         59 |              59 |
| `courses/sunlit-shoals.collision.glb` | 28,764 |                  1,608 |          5 |               5 |

Combined GLB size is 193,732 bytes. Both course files contain all five static
solids. `node.extras.reefRush` carries version, role, identifier, category,
primitive dimensions, and game space transform. The exact typed contract and
bounded export profile are in `tools\asset-profile.d.mts` and the existing
`ASSET-LICENSE.md`. Runtime integration consumes the pure shared contract
without importing filesystem based tooling into the browser.

TDD evidence: the implementer observed nine missing output failures, then 47
validation/command failures, and regressions for animation start time and
duplicate source solids. Independent review found missing mandatory animation
input bounds, invalid surface winding, and quaternion sign equivalence gaps.
The parent observed five failing cases before repairing them. A later review
found aliased accessors could expand small files into excessive decoded data.
The parent observed a failing preflight regression before adding the aggregate
limit of 1,000,000 decoded components per GLB.

One generic independent Rubber Duck context per round reviewed spec and
quality, with high effort requested. The final scoped followup cleared the
remaining resource budget finding; earlier full review found no other open
issues. No model diversity or consensus is claimed.

Parent final evidence: all 71 asset tests, types, lint, formatting, and asset
validation pass. A separate headless generator process reproduced all four
SHA256 hashes exactly; its owned temporary output was removed. This establishes
repeatability on the same Blender build/platform, not across versions.

No runtime, course definition, physics, lockfile, or dependencies changed in
the asset generation slice. The later integration below switches Sunlit to GLB.
Human graphics quality and gameplay feel remain unobserved.

### Standalone leased cache checkpoint

`src\game\assets\AssetCache.ts` exports
`createAssetCache({ loader? })`. The injected loader supplies
`loadAsync(url): Promise<{ scene: Group; animations: readonly AnimationClip[] }>`.
The default uses the real Three.js `GLTFLoader`. `acquire('fish/sunfin.glb')`
resolves under `public\assets` through the existing Pages aware `assetUrl`.
Paths must be canonical relative GLB paths, without an `assets/` prefix, URLs,
escapes, traversal, queries, or fragments.

A lease exposes `root`/`scene`, readonly animations, `disposed`,
`getResourceCounts()`, and synchronous retryable `dispose()`. The cache exposes
entry/reservation/resource diagnostics. Consumers own their animation mixers:
stop and uncache them before releasing leases. Geometry, materials, textures,
and animation clips are borrowed immutable. Clone nodes and animation targets
are independent. Never mutate a shared material for a per scene effect.

Pending acquisitions reserve ownership before awaiting the shared load. Last
successful release frees distinct resources and evicts the entry. There is no
idle cache retention or global disposal API; every acquisition promise must be
observed and every resulting lease released by its scene owner.

Failed final disposal quarantines the path until explicit cleanup succeeds.
Incomplete resource discovery retains the template under one shared
`ConstructionCleanupError`, including resources not yet visited. All waiting
callers receive that same owner. Known releases are attempted without implicit
rediscovery or child retry. Explicit retries complete discovery, preserve any
nested cleanup owner, and never re-register resources already released.

The first independent review reproduced partial traversal losing unvisited
geometry, materials, and textures. The parent observed two failing regressions
before repair; the implementer added repeated discovery and nested ownership
regressions before extending the fix. Final independent spec and quality review
cleared the standalone cache with no Important or Critical findings. This used
one generic Rubber Duck context per round with high effort requested, not
model diversity or consensus.

Parent final evidence: 54 focused cache tests, types, lint, and formatting pass.
Tests use the actual loader with committed GLB responses and real
`AnimationMixer` instances, plus injected failures. This does not establish
browser integration by itself. No course, SceneRuntime, GameHost, renderer,
dependency, or lockfile changes were made in the standalone cache commit.
The later integration uses this ownership API.

| Field      | Standalone cache experiment                                                                                                                                     |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline   | Four validated assets, no runtime cache or shared lease owner.                                                                                                  |
| Hypothesis | Reservations and explicit leases can deduplicate loads without losing ownership during partial failures.                                                        |
| Metric     | Independent animated instances; one shared load; exact resource release; zero entries after successful cleanup; retained owner and blocked reuse after failure. |
| Result     | All 54 focused cases pass, including the reproduced discovery loss and its repair. Independent spec and quality gate cleared.                                   |
| Decision   | Keep `a0492a3`; original scenes now consume these leases through their own cleanup boundary.                                                                    |

### Dependency recovery procedure

The manual workflow was added in `04f87ca`. Hosted run `33918334806` reached the
public registry, but installation failed with HTTP 404 for the lockfile's
`wrap-ansi-cjs-7.0.0.tgz` URL. Neither cache export nor validation ran.

CI work.

   read access to repository contents, official actions pinned by commit,
   Node selected from `.nvmrc`, and a 20 minute job limit.
2. Run `npm ci --no-audit --no-fund` on `windows-2022` without changing the
   dependency versions or registry. Export only `_cacache`, not logs, configuration,
   credentials, or the workspace. Retain the artifact for two days.

--cache <cache-directory>` with the pinned runtime. npm still enforces the

   Preserve all completed milestone regressions before beginning the next task.

## Hill climbing record

### Task 5 runtime investigation

Hosted baseline `33919453978` on `a9bd186` passed completely. The new collision
regressions then produced 16 expected failures with the three original movement
integration cases still passing. They cover thin barriers, initial and changed
rotations, collider removal and filters, live translation, parent transforms,
fish offsets, and live trigger geometry.

Replacing snapshots with live Rapier reads exposed a deeper loading defect:
the existing bundler package and file alias mix native WebAssembly callbacks
with Vitest's transformed JavaScript binding. Even `Collider.translation()`
fails, independently reproduced in `tests\integration\rapierRuntime.test.ts`.
Native Node import also fails on the package's extensionless ESM imports.

Preparation run `33920215947` generated npm metadata and cache for the official
`@dimforge/rapier3d-compat` build at the same `0.20.0` engine version. The complete
dependency diff changed only that package and its direct root entry; unrelated
versions and integrity hashes stayed unchanged. Local metadata matches those
npm generated artifacts exactly, and offline installation succeeded.

The compatible package, serialized initialization, and removal of the Vite file
alias made the isolated binding test pass. The temporary preparation mode was
removed from the workflow. Ordinary dispatch still performs the full baseline.

## Task 6 implementation checkpoint

Goal: load validated course data into an owned physics lifecycle without
coupling it to React or creating the playable renderer early.

Design decisions:

- Use the installed Zod package for version 1 course definitions and inferred
  TypeScript types. Require finite vectors, positive dimensions, valid
  rotations, unique object IDs, a spawn, ordered checkpoint data, optional
  pearl data, and generated visual metadata. Reject malformed data before
  allocating physics resources.
- Keep geometry and obstacles declarative. Support static boxes and spheres,
  bounded current volumes, and rotating solid gates. Reuse gameplay collision
  groups and the compatible Rapier runtime; do not reintroduce snapshots.
- Use an explicit dynamic import registry. Initially register only generated
  Sunlit Shoals. Known courses not built yet, unknown IDs, malformed modules,
  and import failures must produce clear errors rather than silent substitutes.
- A course runtime owns only its colliders, bodies, and obstacles, never the
  caller's physics world. Disposal is idempotent. Construction failure unwinds
  all allocations, including partial obstacle construction, then propagates
  the error. Do not delete unrelated world objects.
- Currents add together inside inclusive box volumes and contribute zero
  outside. Gate updates use bounded accumulated angles and publish live
  kinematic transforms before fish queries. Invalid time values fail clearly;
  zero time does not advance the gate.
- Keep Sunlit Shoals generated. The other two course layouts, progression,
  scene assembly, art, and audio remain in their later tasks.

Implementation order and intended files:

1. `src\game\course\courseDefinition.ts` and
   `tests\unit\courseDefinition.test.ts`: validation and inferred types.
2. `src\content\courses\sunlitShoals.ts`,
   `src\game\course\loadCourseDefinition.ts`, and
   `tests\unit\loadCourseDefinition.test.ts`: one honest generated course and
   explicit loading errors with no eager course import.
3. `src\game\obstacles\Obstacle.ts`, `CurrentVolume.ts`, and `RotatingGate.ts`,
   with focused current and gate tests: update, sampling, and disposal
   contracts independent of React and rendering.
4. `src\game\course\createCourseRuntime.ts` and
   `tests\integration\courseRuntime.test.ts`: allocation, update, sampling,
   cleanup, and rollback using real Rapier worlds.
5. Observe failing regressions before implementing each behavior, then run the
   relevant existing Vitest runner, type checking, and linting. Preserve the
   Task 5 physics regressions. One independent Rubber Duck gate checks spec
   compliance first and code quality second before any commit or push.

Acceptance: malformed definitions allocate nothing; valid generated Sunlit
Shoals loads dynamically; currents and gate poses are deterministic; repeated
course disposal and failed construction return collider and body counts to
the caller's baseline while unrelated objects survive. The full milestone gate
must pass before Task 7. Human playability and visual quality remain unobserved.

## Task 7 implementation checkpoint

Goal: add deterministic race rules and durable local progression without
coupling them to physics ownership, React, or rendering.

Design decisions:

- Add ordered positive `medalTimesMs` thresholds to the course schema. Sunlit
  initially uses gold 12,000 ms, silver 18,000 ms, and bronze 30,000 ms.
  These are provisional content values, not a human calibration result.
- A pure `RaceSession` has ready, running, paused, and finished states, guarded
  start/pause/resume transitions, immutable snapshots, and events. Elapsed time
  uses milliseconds to match the existing shell, while steps accept seconds.
  Paused or finished races neither accumulate time nor collect objects.
- Checkpoint array order defines traversal and the last checkpoint is the
  finish. Detect forward crossings of each checkpoint's oriented disk using
  the actual movement segment, not endpoint overlap. Several checkpoints may
  advance in one step only in both route and segment order.
- Sweep pearl spheres against movement, with a configurable fish radius and a
  default of 0.35 units. Collect each ID once per race. Clip pickup collection
  and elapsed time to the finish fraction so a long frame cannot collect items
  or charge time beyond the finish.
- Award medals inclusively at their thresholds. Preserve best time, highest
  awarded medal, and best pearl count independently across runs. Unlock Sunlit
  initially, Kelpworks after a Sunlit bronze or better, and Blacksmoker after
  a Kelpworks bronze or better with the preceding progression chain intact.
  Content availability remains separate from unlock eligibility.
- Use a strict immutable version 1 progress schema and the stable storage key
  `reef-rush.progress`. Unknown versions, malformed data, denied storage access,
  and quota failures must be explicit. Never automatically overwrite or remove
  an invalid save. An empty save can return empty progress without writing it.
  Task 13 owns the recovery UI.
- Access browser storage lazily and allow dependency injection for tests,
  including failure to obtain the storage object itself. Keep persisted data
  limited to progression; settings and audio remain Task 10.

Implementation order and intended files:

1. `src\game\race\medals.ts`, course schema/data/fixture updates, and
   `tests\unit\medals.test.ts`: threshold validation and awards.
2. `src\game\race\RaceSession.ts`, optional focused race types/geometry modules,
   and `tests\unit\RaceSession.test.ts`: crossing geometry, active timing,
   pickups, state transitions, immutable events and results.
3. `src\game\progression\progress.ts` and `tests\unit\progress.test.ts`:
   validated best records and derived unlocks without regressions.
4. `src\game\save\progressStorage.ts` and
   `tests\unit\progressStorage.test.ts`: real Storage round trips and explicit
   invalid, unsupported, unavailable, and failed write paths.
5. Observe failing assertions before implementing each behavior. Preserve
   course and physics tests, then run the full milestone gate and one
   independent Rubber Duck review before committing or pushing.

Acceptance: no checkpoint skips or reverse crossing awards; no missed pickups
from a fast segment; exact active time and finish interpolation; no repeated
finish events or pickups; inclusive medal boundaries; best records and unlocks
never regress; corrupt or unavailable storage does not silently erase progress.
Task 7 did not change GameHost, React, or rendering; Task 8 provides that
integration. Additional courses and recovery UI remain in later tasks.

| Hypothesis | Retaining exact intersection and clock information until output conversion makes boundary outcomes consistent without gameplay tolerances.                                                                                                                         |
| Acceptance | Ordered forward crossings, inclusive tangencies, earliest pickups, exact finish clipping, equivalent medal outcomes across 24 through 240 Hz and unequal partitions, rejection of genuinely slower controls, monotonic records, and preservation of invalid saves. |

## Task 8 runtime checkpoint

**Goal:** make generated Sunlit playable through the existing shell without
moving simulation state into React or claiming finished art.

**Architecture:** `SceneRuntime` owns the physics world, course, fish, race,
generated scene graph, and chase camera. `GameHost` owns asynchronous loading,
the renderer, input, resize observation, animation scheduling, and the bridge
to the app store. React owns navigation and a HUD updated at most ten times per
second, with immediate terminal transitions.

Use the existing direct Three.js architecture rather than adding a React
renderer library. Reject a single monolithic host because resource ownership
and cancellation need isolated tests. Preserve the warm sand, turquoise water,
and coral palette; generated route rings and the fish must be legible against
the water. This is a playable blockout, not the Task 9 asset milestone.

Important surfaces:

- `src\game\core\SceneRuntime.ts`, `sceneRuntimeTuning.ts`, and
  `src\game\rendering\createGeneratedSceneVisuals.ts`
- `src\game\core\GameHost.ts`, `exposeGameHost.ts`, and `fixedStep.ts`
- `src\app\App.tsx`, `appStore.ts`, `screens.ts`, and runtime screen components
- `src\game\input\InputController.ts` and `InputFrame.ts`
- `src\game\progression\progress.ts` and protected storage helpers
- `playwright.config.ts`, `tests\browser\`, and scene/host/unit regressions

### Task 8 owned scene slice

Commit `7f10ed5` adds `SceneRuntime.ts`, `sceneRuntimeTuning.ts`, generated
visual ownership, and matching integration tests. It also repairs the camera
viewing axis, retry behavior after failed physics disposal, and reciprocal
overflow in controller velocity calculation. Each repair followed a failing
regression.

`createSceneRuntime(definition, dependencies?)` creates a ready race and takes
exclusive ownership of its physics world. It exposes scene/camera references,
immutable snapshots and diagnostics, guarded start/pause/resume, `step`,
`present`, and retryable `dispose`. It does not create a renderer or save data.

Scene simulation requires normalized finite control axes in `[-1, 1]`, actual
boolean action flags, and dt in `[0, 0.1]` seconds. Invalid calls reject before
pause or any simulation mutation. The pure RaceSession contract is unchanged.
Small accepted timesteps use component division rather than an overflowing
reciprocal. Subsequent normal steps and camera presentation remain finite.

The host must retain a failed scene for a later `dispose()` attempt. If scene
construction throws `ConstructionCleanupError`, retain the error until
`retryCleanup()` or `dispose()` succeeds. Child cleanup precedes world release;
independent visual cleanup still runs. The first catch does not implicitly
retry a child's failed rollback.

| Field      | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| ---------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Baseline   | `e4aac69`: real Three.js types and 669 Vitest tests pass; no owned scene runtime.                                                                                                                                                       |
| Hypothesis | Composing the accepted physics, course, race, and camera components behind explicit ownership enables a complete generated route without React or WebGL dependencies.                                                                   |
| Acceptance | Actual steer/throttle traversal reaches all checkpoints and pearls; live visuals match physics; pause/finish freeze simulation; repeated and failed cleanup preserve retry ownership; rejected input leaves live state unchanged.       |
| Result     | Real Sunlit completes in 1,317 fixed steps at 60 Hz, 21,940.483 ms, four checkpoints, four pearls, bronze. No teleport, forced finish, dash assistance, or medal edits. Parent full validation passes 770 Vitest and 3 bootstrap tests. |
| Decision   | Keep. The independent scene review cleared spec and quality with zero Important or Critical findings. Hosted baseline `33935734607` also passed; the host and browser integration is recorded below.                                    |

Review rounds used one independent generic context each, the default model,
and high effort requested. No model diversity or consensus is claimed. These
scene results alone do not establish browser rendering, GPU driver reclamation,
human visual quality, or gameplay feel.

### Task 8 host and browser slice

`GameHost` is owned by main, not a transient screen. `setContainer` is a stable
React ref; detachment suspends the surface without abandoning its retry owner.
Only one canvas and animation chain may exist. Loading checks generations
after asynchronous boundaries; late resources are released, not attached.
`whenIdle()` and `dispose()` wait for loading and queued persistence. Failed
cleanup remains reachable through the host and `retryCleanup()`; new loading
is blocked until cleanup succeeds.

The fixed loop uses 1/60 second steps. Pause or finish aborts remaining catchup;
resume resets the wall timestamp and accumulator. Focus is checked before
attaching the canvas. Loading into an unfocused document pauses at zero time,
and focus return alone does not resume. Escape works on focused pause controls
without stealing native Space/Enter activation or editable input behavior.

Finishes display results immediately. Persistence takes the native Web Lock
`reef-rush.progress`, then synchronously reads current storage, merges it with
the latest session records, and writes through the protected storage helper.
`mergeProgress` takes minimum best time, maximum medal, and maximum pearl count
independently across the union of courses. Invalid current raw data remains
untouched. Missing coordination, unavailable storage, and write failures leave
session results visible with an explicit notice. Saving continues across title
navigation and disposal, but a blocked lock can delay awaiters.

Test builds expose only immutable observation through
`window.__REEF_RUSH_TEST__.getSnapshot()`. There are no teleport, clock, finish,
or input mutation actions. Normal production removes the hook. Three.js,
Rapier, course loading, and scene chunks remain lazy until a course is requested.

Review repairs covered stale saved records, loading focus loss, Escape on
buttons, and pointer movement through HUD/status panels. A browser image also
exposed HUD obstruction; the measured lower HUD edge was 50.6% down the canvas.
The compact desktop HUD now stays within the top 25% at 1280x900 and 1024x768,
leaving the central fish/route view uncovered. These repairs had failing
regressions before implementation; this is a visibility metric, not an art score.

Parent browser observations on the final candidate:

| Run                                 | Elapsed ms | Fixed steps | Checkpoints | Pearls | Medal  |
| ----------------------------------- | ---------- | ----------- | ----------- | ------ | ------ |
| Queued save and newer valid records | 23,373.098 | 1,403       | 4 / 4       | 4 / 4  | Bronze |
| Invalid existing storage            | 23,007.886 | 1,381       | 4 / 4       | 4 / 4  | Bronze |

The driver reads snapshots and issues actual Playwright keyboard input.
Reduced rendering resolution is used for driver stability, not modified
simulation or timing. Other saved records in the coordination scenario are
explicit fixtures, not claimed gameplay achievements.

All 11 browser scenarios pass: HUD/status input isolation, desktop visibility,
native hidden-tab pause, delayed loading while hidden, real keyboard completion,
queued and invalid storage, repeated lifecycle, failure recovery, cancellation,
and the separate normal production build. Three reload cycles retain one body,
seven colliders, four geometries, and fifteen materials while active. Title
teardown shows no canvas or animation owner, no scene, no pending cleanup, and
no idle frame advancement.

| Field      | Evidence                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Baseline   | `9fad19c`: 770 Vitest tests pass; the scene is not connected to the application.                                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| Hypothesis | Explicit host ownership, isolated input, and coordinated persistence can expose the accepted simulation through the real browser without coupling React to frame state or losing progress.                                                                                                 |
| Acceptance | Real keyboard completion and matching results; pause exclusion and cancellation; stable repeated resource counts; no newer-record loss or invalid-save overwrite; normal production has no diagnostics.                                                                                    |
| Result     | Parent `npm run test:browser`: 11 passed. Parent `npm run validate`: 861 Vitest and 3 bootstrap tests, formatting, lint, types, asset validation, and build passed. Hosted Windows baseline `33940260314` passed on `2bffc2f`. Final independent spec and quality review cleared the gate. |
| Decision   | Keep `2bffc2f` as the playable baseline. Task 9 preserves these acceptance gates with original assets.                                                                                                                                                                                     |

Limits: native focus evidence covers hidden tabs, not visible but unfocused
windows. Failed ESM import recovery uses a real page reload; automatic recovery
remains Task 13. Hard process exit and writers that do not honor the lock are
not durability guarantees. Headless evidence is not human gameplay feel or
visual quality. GPU driver reclamation is not inferred from object counters.
The existing large Three.js/Rapier chunk warning remains visible. Browser CI,
broader compatibility, quality presets, and deployment remain later tasks.

`npm run test:browser` owns strict preview ports 4173/4174 and isolated test and
normal builds. Screenshots/traces use unique OS temporary directories outside
the repository. `npm run validate` leaves the normal production build in `dist`.
The manual Windows baseline currently runs validation, not browser scenarios.

At milestone boundaries, preserve decisions and the next action here before
starting a fresh session. Use focused compaction within an experiment. Reference
large local logs rather than pasting them, but retain enough portable evidence
here that another device does not need the original session folder.
